N_{j}^{+}\in{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}^{\prime}
but also for
N_{j}^{+}\in{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}
(full nilpotent radical).

We write

\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})=\mathbb{C}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n}],

where
(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n})
is
the dual coordinates to

(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n-1},z_{n})
of
{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}
, which
corresponds to the coordinates

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n-1},x_{n})
of
{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}(\mathbb{R})
in (
4.13
).
Let

E_{x}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}

and

E_{\zeta}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\zeta_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta_{j}}

denote
the Euler homogeneity operator for
x
and
\zeta
, respectively.
For
j\in\{1,\ldots,n-1,n\}
,
we write
\vartheta_{j}=\zeta_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta_{j}}

for the Euler operator for
\zeta_{j}
such that

E_{\zeta}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\vartheta_{j}
.

Proposition 5.1

(
[
20
, Props. 5.1 and 5.4]
)
.

For
j\in\{1,\ldots,n-1,n\}
, we have

\displaystyle d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}(N_{j}^{+})

\displaystyle=x_{j}\{(n-\lambda)+E_{x}\},

\displaystyle-\zeta_{j}\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{j}^{+})

\displaystyle=\vartheta_{j}(\lambda-1+E_{\zeta}).

(5.2)

Observe that
E_{\zeta}|_{\mathrm{Pol}^{k}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})}
is simply given by

E_{\zeta}|_{\mathrm{Pol}^{k}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})}=k\cdot\mathrm{id}
.
Then, for
j\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
,
we have

-\zeta_{j}\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{j}^{+})|_{\mathrm{Pol}^{k}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})}=(\lambda-1+k)\vartheta_{j}.

(5.3)

5.2.
Step 2:
Classify and construct

\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(W^{\vee},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes V^{\vee})
.

For
(\varpi,W)\in\mathrm{Irr}(M_{0}^{\prime})_{\mathrm{fin}}
, we write

W_{\beta}=\mathbb{C}_{\beta}\otimes W

for
the representation
space of
(\beta,\varpi)\in\mathrm{Irr}(M^{\prime})_{\mathrm{fin}}
, where

\mathbb{C}_{\beta}
denotes the one-dimensional representation

\mathbb{C}_{\beta}=(\mathrm{sgn}^{\beta},\mathbb{C})
of
M^{\prime}
defined as in
(
3.9
).
Similarly, for
\alpha\in\{\pm\}
, we define
the
M
-representation
\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\alpha}
by

\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\alpha}=\mathbb{C}_{\alpha}\otimes\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+}).

(5.4)

In this step, we wish to classify and construct

\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(W_{\beta}^{\vee}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}).

We start by observing the
M^{\prime}A^{\prime}
-decomposition

\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})|_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}=\mathbb{C}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n}]|_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}
.
To do so, the following lemma is useful.

Lemma 5.5
.

The following hold.

(1)

(M^{\prime},\mathrm{Ad}_{\#},\mathbb{C}^{m}[\zeta_{n}])\hskip 42.0pt\simeq(SL^{\pm}(n-1,\mathbb{R}),\mathrm{sgn}^{m}\otimes\mathrm{triv},\mathbb{C})
.

(2)

(M^{\prime},\mathrm{Ad}_{\#},\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1}])\hskip 2.0pt\simeq(SL^{\pm}(n-1,\mathbb{R}),\mathrm{sgn}^{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1},S^{\ell}(\mathbb{C}^{n-1}))
.

(3)

A^{\prime}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{m}[\zeta_{n}]
by a character
(\chi^{\prime})^{-m}
.

(4)

A^{\prime}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1}]
by a character

(\chi^{\prime})^{-\frac{n}{n-1}\ell}
.

Here
\chi^{\prime}
is the character of
A^{\prime}
defined in (
3.8
).

Proof.

A direct computation.
∎

Remark 5.6
.

If
n=2
, then, by (
3.13
), we have

\displaystyle(M^{\prime},\mathrm{Ad}_{\#},\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1}])

\displaystyle\simeq(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z},\mathrm{sgn}^{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{1},S^{\ell}(\mathbb{C}))

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z},\mathrm{triv}\otimes\mathrm{triv},\mathbb{C}).

It follows from Lemma
5.5
that

\mathbb{C}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n}]|_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}
decomposes irreducibly as

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n}]|_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathbb{C}^{m}[\zeta_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1}]

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}(\mathrm{sgn}^{m+\ell}\otimes\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1})\boxtimes(-(m+\tfrac{n}{n-1}\ell)),

(5.7)

where
-(m+\tfrac{n}{n-1}\ell)
indicates the weight of the character of
A^{\prime}
.
Therefore,
we have

\big(\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}\big)|_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}\simeq\bigoplus_{m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}(\mathrm{sgn}^{\alpha+m+\ell}\otimes\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1})\boxtimes(-(\lambda+m+\tfrac{n}{n-1}\ell)).

We remark that the
M^{\prime}A^{\prime}
-representations appeared in (
5.7
) are all inequivalent.

Put

\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}:=\{(\alpha,\alpha+m+\ell;\mathrm{poly}^{\ell}_{n-1};\lambda,\lambda+m+\tfrac{n}{n-1}\ell):\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\;\text{and}\;m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}.

(5.8)

Proposition 5.9
.

The following conditions on

(\alpha,\beta;\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\{\pm\}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n-1,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
are equivalent.

(i)

\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(W^{\vee}_{\beta}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})\neq\{0\}
.

(ii)

\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(W^{\vee}_{\beta}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})=1
.

(iii)

(\alpha,\beta;\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Since
(\mathrm{sym}_{n-1}^{k},S^{k}(\mathbb{C}^{n-1}))^{\vee}\simeq(\mathrm{poly}_{n-1}^{k},\mathrm{Pol}^{k}(\mathbb{C}^{n-1}))
,
the assertions simply follow from the preceding arguments.
∎

Now, for
\mathbf{l}=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{n-1})\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}
, we write

\zeta_{\mathbf{l}}=\zeta_{1}^{\ell_{1}}\cdots\zeta_{n-1}^{\ell_{n-1}}\in\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1}].

We then define

\psi_{(m,\ell)}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(S^{\ell}(\mathbb{C}^{n-1}),\mathbb{C}^{m}[\zeta_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1}])
by

\psi_{(m,\ell)}=\zeta_{n}^{m}\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}\zeta_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}},

(5.10)

where
\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}\in\mathrm{Pol}^{k}(\mathbb{C}^{n-1})\simeq S^{k}(\mathbb{C}^{n-1})^{\vee}
are regarded as
the dual basis of
e_{\mathbf{l}}\in S^{k}(\mathbb{C}^{n-1})
.

Proposition 5.11
.

We have

\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(W_{\beta}^{\vee}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,\ell)}&\text{if
$(\alpha,\beta;\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}$,}\\
\{0\}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

As
\psi_{(m,\ell)}
maps

\psi_{(m,\ell)}\colon e_{\mathbf{l}}\mapsto\zeta_{n}^{m}\zeta_{\mathbf{l}}
,
it satisfies the desired
M^{\prime}A^{\prime}
-equivariance if

(\alpha,\beta;\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}
.
Thus, we have

\psi_{(m,\ell)}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(W_{\beta}^{\vee}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})
.
Now the multiplicity-one property from Proposition
5.9
concludes
the assertion.
∎

5.3.
Step 3:
Solve the F-system
for
\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(W^{\vee},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes V^{\vee})
.

For
(\alpha,\beta;\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n-1,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
, we put

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\alpha,\lambda},\varpi_{\beta,\nu})

\displaystyle:=\{\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(W_{\beta}^{\vee}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}):\text{
$\psi$ solves the F-system \eqref{eqn:Fsys21} below.}\}.

(\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{j}^{+})\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W})\psi=0\quad\text{for all $j\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$}.

(5.12)

Since

\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\alpha,\lambda},\varpi_{\beta,\nu})\subset\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(W_{\beta}^{\vee}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}),

it follows from
Proposition
5.9
that
if
\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\alpha,\lambda},\varpi_{\beta,\nu})\neq\{0\}
,
then
(\alpha,\beta;\varpi;\lambda,\nu)
is of the form

(\alpha,\beta;\varpi;\lambda,\nu)=(\alpha,\alpha+m+\ell;\mathrm{poly}^{\ell}_{n-1};\lambda,\lambda+m+\tfrac{n}{n-1}\ell)

(5.13)

for some
m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Further, by Proposition
5.11
,
it suffices to solve the PDE

(\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{j}^{+})\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W})\psi_{(m,\ell)}=0\quad\text{for all $j\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$},

which is equivalent to solving

(-\zeta_{j}\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{j}^{+})\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W})\psi_{(m,\ell)}=0\quad\text{for all $j\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$}.

(5.14)

Recall from (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) that we have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,1}

\displaystyle=\{(\alpha,\alpha+m;\mathrm{triv};\lambda,\lambda+m):\alpha\in\{\pm\},\lambda\in\mathbb{C},\;\text{and}\;m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\},

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,2}

\displaystyle=\{(\alpha,\alpha+m+\ell;\mathrm{poly}_{n-1}^{\ell};1-(m+\ell),1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}):\alpha\in\{\pm\}\;\text{and}\;\ell,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}.

Theorem 5.15
.

Let
n\geq 3
. We have

\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\alpha,\lambda},\varpi_{\beta,\nu})=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,0)}&\text{if $(\alpha,\beta;\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,1}$,}\\[3.0pt]
\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,\ell)}&\text{if $(\alpha,\beta;\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,2}$,}\\
\{0\}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

We wish to solve (
5.14
).
Recall from (
5.3
) that

-\zeta_{j}\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{j}^{+})|_{\mathrm{Pol}^{k}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})}
is given by

-\zeta_{j}\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{j}^{+})|_{\mathrm{Pol}^{k}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})}=(\lambda-1+k)\vartheta_{j}.

Since

\psi_{(m,\ell)}=\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}\zeta_{n}^{m}\zeta_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}\in\mathrm{Pol}^{m+\ell}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathrm{Pol}^{\ell}(\mathbb{C}^{n-1}),

the left-hand side of (
5.14
) amounts to

\displaystyle(-\zeta_{j}\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{j}^{+})\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W})\psi_{(m,\ell)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}-\zeta_{j}\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{j}^{+})(\zeta_{n}^{m}\zeta_{\mathbf{l}})\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}(\lambda-1+m+\ell)\vartheta_{j}(\zeta_{n}^{m}\zeta_{\mathbf{l}})\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}

\displaystyle=\zeta_{n}^{m}\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}(\lambda-1+m+\ell)\vartheta_{j}(\zeta_{\mathbf{l}})\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}.

Thus, one wishes to solve

(\lambda-1+m+\ell)\vartheta_{j}(\zeta_{\mathbf{l}})=0\quad\text{for all $j\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$ and $\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}$}.

(5.16)

Since

(\lambda-1+m+\ell)\vartheta_{j}(\zeta_{\mathbf{l}})=(\lambda-1+m+\ell)\ell_{j}\zeta_{\mathbf{l}}
,
Equation
(
5.16
) holds if and only if
\ell=0
or
\lambda=1-(m+\ell)
.
Now the theorem follows from (
5.13
)
with the observation that

\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,1}=\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}
for
\ell=0
and

\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,2}=\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}
for
\lambda=1-(m+\ell)
.

∎

5.4.
Step 4:
Apply
\mathrm{Rest}_{x_{n}=0}\circ\mathrm{symb}^{-1}

and

F_{c}^{-1}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W}

to the solution

\psi\in\mathrm{Sol}(\mathfrak{n}_{+};V,W)
.

Observe that

\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}
in (
4.14
) and
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}

in (
4.22
) are
given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Rest}_{x_{n}=0}\circ\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial x_{n}^{m}}\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}\frac{\partial^{\ell}}{\partial x^{\mathbf{l}}}\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Rest}_{x_{n}=0}\circ\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}\mathrm{symb}^{-1}(\zeta_{n}^{m}\zeta_{\mathbf{l}})\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Rest}_{x_{n}=0}\circ\mathrm{symb}^{-1}(\psi_{(m,\ell)})

and

\Phi_{(m,\ell)}=(N_{n}^{-})^{m}\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}N_{\mathbf{l}}^{-}\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}=\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}F_{c}^{-1}(\zeta_{n}^{m}\zeta_{\mathbf{l}})\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}=(F_{c}^{-1}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W})(\psi_{(m,\ell)}).

Now we are ready to prove Theorems
4.10
,
4.15
,
4.20
,
and
4.23
.

Proof of
Theorems
4.10
,
4.15
,
4.20
,
and
4.23
.

By Theorem
2.20
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Diff}_{G^{\prime}}(I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha},I(\varpi,\nu)^{\beta})

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{Rest}_{x_{n}=0}\circ\mathrm{symb}^{-1})(\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\alpha,\lambda},\varpi_{\beta,\nu})),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},P^{\prime}}(M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\varpi^{\vee},-\nu)^{\beta},M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},-\lambda)^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=(F_{c}^{-1}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W})(\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\alpha,\lambda},\varpi_{\beta,\nu})).

Since
(\mathrm{poly}_{n-1}^{\ell})^{\vee}\simeq\mathrm{sym}_{n-1}^{\ell}
,
the proposed assertions follow from Theorem
5.15
.
∎

We end this section by discussing the proof of Theorem
4.26
.

Proof of Theorem
4.26
.

Let
P_{0}^{\prime}=M_{0}^{\prime}A^{\prime}N_{+}^{\prime}
be a Langlands decomposition of
the identity component of the parabolic subgroup
P^{\prime}=M^{\prime}A^{\prime}N_{+}^{\prime}
.
For
(\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n-1,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
,
we let

\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\varpi_{\nu})_{0}=\{\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{M_{0}^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(W^{\vee}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}):\text{
$\psi$ solves the F-system \eqref{eqn:Fsys2}.}\}.

As
P_{0}^{\prime}
is connected, we have

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},P_{0}^{\prime}}\big(M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\sigma,r),M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},s)\big)=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}\big(M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\sigma,r),M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},s)\big),

which yields a linear isomorphism

F_{c}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W}\colon\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}(M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\varpi^{\vee},-\nu),M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},-\lambda))\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\varpi_{\nu})_{0}.

(5.17)

Now Theorem
4.26
follows from the same arguments
in Sections
5.1
–
5.4
.
∎

For later convenience, we state
the classification of
\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\varpi_{\nu})_{0}
.
Put

\displaystyle\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,1}

\displaystyle:=\{(\mathrm{triv};\lambda,\lambda+m):\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\;\text{and}\;m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\},

\displaystyle\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,2}

\displaystyle:=\{(\mathrm{poly}^{\ell}_{n-1};1-(m+\ell),1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}):m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}.

Proposition 5.18
.

We have

\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\varpi_{\nu})_{0}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,0)}&\text{if $(\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,1}$,}\\[3.0pt]
\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,\ell)}&\text{if $(\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,2}$,}\\
\{0\}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

Since
the arguments are identical to Theorem
5.15
,
we omit the proof.
∎

6.
Proofs for the classification and construction of
\mathbb{D}
and
\Phi
:
Case
n=2

The aim of this section is to prove the theorems in Section
4

for the case
n=2
, namely,
Theorems
4.11
,
4.16
,
4.21
,

4.24
, and
4.27
.
Throughout this section we assume
n=2
; in particular, we have

(G,G^{\prime})=(SL(3,\mathbb{R}),SL(2,\mathbb{R}))
and

\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})=\mathbb{C}[\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}]
.

Observe that since
G^{\prime}=SL(2,\mathbb{R})
, the
M^{\prime}
-part of
the parabolic subgroup
P^{\prime}=M^{\prime}A^{\prime}N_{+}^{\prime}
of
G^{\prime}
is given by
M^{\prime}\simeq\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
;
thus,

\mathrm{Irr}(P^{\prime})_{\mathrm{fin}}\simeq\{\pm\}\times\{\mathrm{triv}\}\times\mathbb{C}.

So, the space of solutions to the F-system in concern is

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\alpha,\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\beta,\nu})=\{\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{\beta}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}):\text{
$\psi$ solves \eqref{eqn:Fsys3} below.}\}.

\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{1}^{+})\psi=0.

(6.1)

In the present case, as opposed to the case
n\geq 3
,
the space
\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{\beta}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})

could have higher multiplicity.
Thus,
to simplify the exposition,
we first follow the recipe of the F-method for

P_{0}^{\prime}=M_{0}^{\prime}A^{\prime}N_{+}^{\prime}=A^{\prime}N_{+}^{\prime}
. We shall consider the parity condition coming from

M^{\prime}
in the end.

As in the previous section, we put

\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\nu})_{0}:=\{\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}):\text{
$\psi$ solves \eqref{eqn:Fsys3}.}\}.

Then we shall proceed with the following steps.

Step 1

Classify and construct

\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})
.

Step 2

Solve (
6.1
)
for
\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})
.

Step 3

Consider the parity condition on

\psi\in\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\nu})_{0}
.

Step 4

Apply
\mathrm{Rest}_{x_{2}=0}\circ\mathrm{symb}^{-1}

and

F_{c}^{-1}

to the solution
\psi\in\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\alpha,\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\beta,\nu})
.

As the identity component
P_{0}^{\prime}
is considered,
our arguments naturally include the proof of
Theorem
4.27
as in the end of the previous section.

6.1.
Step 1:
Classify and construct

\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})
.

It follows from Lemma
5.5
that we have

\mathbb{C}^{m}[\zeta_{2}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1}]\simeq-(m+2\ell),

(6.2)

where
-(m+2\ell)
indicates the weight of the character of
A^{\prime}
.

Proposition 6.3
.

The following conditions on

(\lambda,\nu)\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
are equivalent.

(i)

\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})\neq\{0\}
.

(ii)

\nu-\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Proof.

By (
6.2
),
the decomposition

\mathbb{C}[\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}]|_{A^{\prime}}
is given as

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}]|_{A^{\prime}}=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\bigoplus_{m+2\ell=k}\mathbb{C}^{m}[\zeta_{2}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1}]\simeq\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\bigoplus_{m+2\ell=k}(-k).

Therefore,

\big(\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}\big)|_{A^{\prime}}\simeq\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\bigoplus_{m+2\ell=k}-(\lambda+k),

which shows the proposed assertion.
∎

As in (
5.10
),
we write

\psi_{(m,\ell)}=\zeta_{2}^{m}\zeta_{1}^{\ell}\in\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}].

Then,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{-(\lambda+k)},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})

\displaystyle=\text{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\psi_{(m,\ell)}:m+2\ell=k\}

(6.4)

\displaystyle=\text{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\psi_{(k-2\ell,\ell)}:\ell=0,\ldots,[\tfrac{k}{2}]\}.

(6.5)

In particular, we have

\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{-(\lambda+k)},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})=[\tfrac{k}{2}]+1.

6.2.
Step 2:
Solve (
6.1
)
for
\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})
.

The aim of this step is to determine
\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\nu})_{0}
,
that is,
we wish to solve

\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{1}^{+})\psi=0,

which is equivalent to solving

-\zeta_{1}\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{1}^{+})\psi=0.

We put

\displaystyle\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,1}

\displaystyle:=\{(\lambda,\lambda+m):\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\;\text{and}\;m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\},

\displaystyle\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,2}

\displaystyle:=\{(1-(m+\ell),1+\ell):m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\},

\displaystyle\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,+}

\displaystyle:=\{(1-(m+\ell),1+\ell):m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\;\text{and}\;\ell\in 1+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}.

We have

\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,+}\subset\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,2}\subset\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,1}.

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
n=2
. We have

\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\nu})_{0}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,0)}&\text{if $(\lambda,\nu)\in\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,1}\setminus\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,+}$, }\\
\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m+2\ell,0)}\oplus\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,\ell)}&\text{if $(\lambda,\nu)\in\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,+}$,}\\
\{0\}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof.

As

\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\nu})_{0}\subset\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{-\nu},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})
,
it follows from Proposition
6.3
that if

\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\nu})_{0}\neq\{0\}
, then

\nu-\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Let
\nu-\lambda=k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
It then follows from (
6.4
) that

\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{-(\lambda+k)},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})

is of the form

\psi=\sum_{m+2\ell=k}c_{\ell}\,\psi_{(m,\ell)}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{[k/2]}c_{\ell}\,\psi_{(k-2\ell,\ell)}

for some
c_{\ell}\in\mathbb{C}
.
Since
\psi_{(k-2\ell,\ell)}=\zeta_{2}^{k-2\ell}\zeta_{1}^{\ell}\in\mathbb{C}^{k-\ell}[\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}]
,
by (
5.3
), we have

\displaystyle-\zeta_{1}\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{1}^{+})\psi

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{[k/2]}c_{\ell}\,(-\zeta_{1}\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{1}^{+})\psi_{(k-2\ell,\ell)})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{[k/2]}c_{\ell}\,(\lambda-1+k-\ell)\vartheta_{1}(\psi_{(k-2\ell,\ell)})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{[k/2]}c_{\ell}\,\ell(\lambda-1+k-\ell)\psi_{(k-2\ell,\ell)}.

As the degrees of
\psi_{(k-2\ell,\ell)}
are all different, the polynomials

\psi_{(k-2\ell,\ell)}
are linearly independent.
Therefore,

-\zeta_{1}\widehat{d\pi_{\lambda^{*}}}(N_{1}^{+})\psi=0

if and only if

c_{\ell}\,\ell(\lambda-1+k-\ell)=0\quad\text{for all $\ell\in\{0,1,\ldots,[\tfrac{k}{2}]\}$},

which
is further equivalent to the following conditions:

(I)

c_{\ell}=0
for
\ell\neq 0
, or

(II)

\lambda=1-(k-\ell_{0})
for some
\ell_{0}
and
c_{j}=0
for
j\neq 0,\ell_{0}
.

First, suppose that Case (I) holds.
If
\ell=0
, then
k=m+2\cdot 0=m
. Therefore,

\psi=\sum_{\ell=0}^{[k/2]}c_{\ell}\,\psi_{(k-2\ell,\ell)}=c_{0}\,\psi_{(k,0)}=c_{0}\,\psi_{(m,0)}.

Since
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
can be any complex number, we have

\psi_{(m,0)}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{-(\lambda+m)},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda})\quad\text{for all $\lambda\in\mathbb{C}$}.

Since
m=k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
is arbitrary, this shows that
if
(\lambda,\nu)=(\lambda,\lambda-m)\in\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,1}
, then

\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,0)}\subset\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\nu})_{0}.

Next, suppose that Case (II) holds.
Write
m_{0}=k-2\ell_{0}
. Then,

\psi=\sum_{\ell=0}^{[k/2]}c_{\ell}\,\psi_{(k-2\ell,\ell)}=c_{0}\,\psi_{(k,0)}+c_{\ell_{0}}\,\psi_{(k-2\ell_{0},\ell_{0})}=c_{0}\,\psi_{(m_{0}+2\ell_{0},0)}+c_{\ell_{0}}\,\psi_{(m_{0},\ell_{0})}.

Since
\lambda=1-(k-\ell_{0})=1-(m_{0}+\ell_{0})
and
\lambda+k=1+\ell_{0}
,
we have

\psi_{(m_{0}+2\ell_{0},0)},\psi_{(m_{0},\ell_{0})}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}_{-(1+\ell_{0})},\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-(1-(m_{0}+\ell_{0}))}).

As for Case (I), since
k=m_{0}+\ell_{0}
is arbitrary, this shows that
if
(\lambda,\nu)=(1-(m+\ell),1+\ell)\in\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,+}
,
then

\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m+2\ell,0)}\oplus\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,\ell)}\subset\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\nu})_{0}.

Since

\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,+}\subset\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,2}\subset\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,1}
,
it follows from the arguments on (I) and (II) that

\dim\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\nu})_{0}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $(\lambda,\nu)\in\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,1}\setminus\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,+}$},\\
2&\text{if $(\lambda,\nu)\in\accentset{\circ}{\Lambda}^{(3,2)}_{SL,+}$},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

This concludes the proposition.
∎

6.3.
Step 3:
Consider the parity condition on

\psi\in\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\nu})_{0}
.

Recall from (
4.4
) and (
4.6
) that
we have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(3,2)}_{SL,1}

\displaystyle=\{(\alpha,\alpha+m;\mathrm{triv};\lambda,\lambda+m):\alpha\in\{\pm\},\lambda\in\mathbb{C},\text{and}\;m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\},

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(3,2)}_{SL,+}

\displaystyle=\{(\alpha,\alpha+m;\mathrm{triv};1-(m+\ell),1+\ell):\alpha\in\{\pm\},m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},\;\text{and}\;\ell\in 1+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}.

Proposition 6.7
.

We have

\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\alpha,\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\beta,\nu})=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,0)}&\text{if $(\lambda,\nu)\in\Lambda^{(3,2)}_{SL,1}\setminus\Lambda^{(3,2)}_{SL,+}$, }\\
\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m+2\ell,0)}\oplus\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,\ell)}&\text{if $(\lambda,\nu)\in\Lambda^{(3,2)}_{SL,+}$,}\\
\{0\}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof.

For
n=2
,
it follows from Lemma
5.5
and Remark
5.6
that

M^{\prime}
acts on
\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,0)}=\mathbb{C}\zeta_{2}^{m}
and

\mathbb{C}\psi_{(m,\ell)}=\mathbb{C}\zeta_{2}^{m}\zeta_{1}^{\ell}

by
\mathrm{sgn}^{m}\otimes\mathrm{triv}
and

\mathrm{sgn}^{m+\ell}\otimes\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{1}=\mathrm{sgn}^{m}\otimes\mathrm{triv}
, respectively.
Proposition
6.6
then concludes the assertion.
∎

6.4.
Step 4:
Apply
\mathrm{Rest}_{x_{2}=0}\circ\mathrm{symb}^{-1}

and

F_{c}^{-1}

to the solution
\psi\in\mathrm{Sol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+};\mathrm{triv}_{\alpha,\lambda},\mathrm{triv}_{\beta,\nu})

Now we finish the proof of the theorems in concern.

Proof of
Theorems
4.11
,
4.16
,
4.21
,

4.24
, and
4.27
.

As in Section
5.4
, we apply
\mathrm{Rest}_{x_{2}=0}\circ\mathrm{symb}^{-1}
and
F_{c}^{-1}

to the polynomial solutions
\psi
in Proposition
6.7
to
obtain
Theorems
4.11
,
4.16
,
4.21
,
and
4.24
.
The application of
F_{c}^{-1}
to
\psi
in Proposition
6.6

concludes Theorem
4.27
. This ends the proof.
∎

7.
Factorization identities of
\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}
and
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}

The aim of this section is to show the factorization identities of
differential symmetry breaking operators
\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}

and
({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},P^{\prime})
-homomorphisms
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}
.
Such factorization identities are obtained in
Theorem
7.12
for
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}
and
Theorem
7.18
for
\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}
.
Throughout this section we assume
n\geq 2
, unless otherwise stated.

7.1.
G
-intertwining differential operators
\mathcal{D}_{k}

and
({\mathfrak{g}},P)
-homomorphisms
\varphi_{k}

As preliminaries,
we first recall from
[
20
]
the classification of
G
-intertwining differential
operators

\mathcal{D}\in\mathrm{Diff}_{G}(I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha},I(\xi,\tau)^{\delta})

and
({\mathfrak{g}},P)
-homomorphisms

\varphi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathfrak{g}},P}(M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\tau,u)^{\delta},M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},s)^{\alpha}).

We define
\Lambda^{n+1}_{SL}\subset\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
by means of

\Lambda^{n+1}_{SL}:=\{(\alpha,\alpha+k;\mathrm{poly}_{n}^{k};1-k,1+\tfrac{k}{n}):\alpha\in\{\pm\}\;\text{and}\;k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}.

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we set

\Xi_{k}:=\{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n-1},k_{n})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{n}:\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k\}

and define
\mathcal{D}_{k}\in\mathrm{Diff}_{\mathbb{C}}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\otimes\mathbb{C}^{k}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{n-1},y_{n}])
as

\mathcal{D}_{k}:=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\Xi_{k}}\frac{\partial^{k}}{\partial x^{\mathbf{k}}}\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{k}}.

(7.1)

For
k=0
, we understand
\mathcal{D}_{0}
as the identity operator
\mathcal{D}_{0}=\mathrm{id}
.

Remark 7.2
.

The differential operator
\mathcal{D}_{k}
can also be expressed as follows.
For
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in\Xi_{k}
with

\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n-1},k_{n})
and

\mathbf{k}^{\prime}=(k_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,k_{n-1}^{\prime},k_{n}^{\prime})
,
write

\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}=(k_{1}+k_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,k_{n-1}+k_{n-1}^{\prime},k_{n}+k_{n}^{\prime}).

We then define
a multiplication on

\mathbb{C}[\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}},\ldots,\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x_{n-1}},\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x_{n}}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{n-1},y_{n}]

by

(\frac{\partial^{k}}{\partial x^{\mathbf{k}}}\otimes y_{\mathbf{k}})\cdot(\frac{\partial^{k}}{\partial x^{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}\otimes y_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}})=\frac{\partial^{2k}}{\partial x^{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}}\otimes y_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}.

Then
\mathcal{D}_{k}
can be given by

\mathcal{D}_{k}=\frac{1}{k!}\big(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}\otimes y_{j}\big)^{k}.

Theorem 7.3

(
[
20
, Thm. 4.5]
)
.

For
(\alpha,\delta;\xi;\lambda,\tau)\in\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n,\mathbb{R}))\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
,
we have

\mathrm{Diff}_{G}(I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha},I(\xi,\tau)^{\delta})=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{id}&\text{if $(\delta,\xi,\tau)=(\alpha,\mathrm{triv},\lambda)$,}\\
\mathbb{C}\mathcal{D}_{k}&\text{if $(\alpha,\delta;\xi;\lambda,\tau)\in\Lambda^{n+1}_{SL}$,}\\
\{0\}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

For
({\mathfrak{g}},P)
-homomorphisms
\varphi
,
we first define
\Lambda^{n+1}_{({\mathfrak{g}},P)}\subset\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(\mathfrak{sl}(n,\mathbb{C}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
by

\Lambda^{n+1}_{({\mathfrak{g}},P)}:=\{(\alpha,\alpha+k;\mathrm{sym}_{n}^{k};k-1,-(1+\tfrac{k}{n})):\alpha\in\{\pm\}\;\text{and}\;k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}.

(7.4)

We define
\varphi_{k}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(S^{k}(\mathbb{C}^{n}),S^{k}({\mathfrak{n}}_{-}))
by means of

\varphi_{k}:=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\Xi_{k}}N_{\mathbf{k}}^{-}\otimes(e_{\mathbf{k}})^{\vee}=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\Xi_{k}}N_{\mathbf{k}}^{-}\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{k}}.

(7.5)

Theorem 7.6

(
[
20
, Thm. 4.8]
)
.

For

(\alpha,\delta;\tau;s,u)\in\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
,
we have

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathfrak{g}},P}(M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\tau,u)^{\delta},M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},s)^{\alpha})=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{id}&\text{if $(\delta,\tau,u)=(\alpha,\mathrm{triv},s)$,}\\
\mathbb{C}\varphi_{k}&\text{if $(\alpha,\delta,\tau;s,u)\in\Lambda^{n+1}_{({\mathfrak{g}},P)}$,}\\
\{0\}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

As for
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}
in (
4.25
), we regard

\varphi_{k}
as a map

\varphi_{k}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathfrak{g}},P}(M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\tau,u)^{\delta},M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},s)^{\alpha})

defined by

\varphi_{k}(u\otimes w):=u\varphi_{k}(w)\quad\text{for $u\in\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{g}})$ and $w\in S^{k}(\mathbb{C}^{n})$.}

(7.7)

In what follows, we write
\mathcal{D}_{k}
and
\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\prime}
for

G
- and
G^{\prime}
-intertwining differential operators, respectively.
The same convention is employed for
({\mathfrak{g}},P)
-homomorphism

\varphi_{k}
and
({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},P^{\prime})
-homomorphism
\varphi_{k}^{\prime}
.

7.2.
Factorization identities for
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}

We first consider the factorization identities of

({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},P^{\prime})
-homomorphisms
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}
.
It is clear from (
4.22
) and (
7.5
) that
the
({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},P^{\prime})
-homomorphism
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}
can be factored as

\Phi_{(m,\ell)}=\Phi_{(m,0)}\circ\varphi_{\ell}^{\prime}.

In other words, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{triv},\ell-1)^{\alpha+m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circlearrowleft}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{(m,0)}}
\textstyle{M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},(m+\ell)-1)^{\alpha}}
\textstyle{M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1},-(1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}))^{\alpha+m+\ell}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\ell}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{(m,\ell)}}

The aim of this subsection is to give another factorization identity of

\Phi_{(m,\ell)}
, that is, we wish to complete the lower left corner
of the following diagram:

\textstyle{M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{triv},\ell-1)^{\alpha+m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circlearrowleft}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{(m,0)}}
\textstyle{M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},(m+\ell)-1)^{\alpha}}
\textstyle{M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1},-(1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}))^{\alpha+m+\ell}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\ell}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{(m,\ell)}}
some generalized Verma module
\scriptstyle{\circlearrowleft}

For this purpose, we introduce some notation.
For
\mathbf{l}=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{n-1})\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}

and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we write

e_{(m,\mathbf{l})}=e_{n}^{m}e_{\mathbf{l}}=e_{n}^{m}e_{1}^{\ell_{1}}\cdots e_{n-1}^{\ell_{n-1}}.

Also, we write

\mathbb{C}[e^{\prime}]=\mathbb{C}[e_{1},\ldots,e_{n-1}]\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{C}[e^{\prime},e_{n}]=\mathbb{C}[e_{1},\ldots,e_{n-1},e_{n}].

Then, for
m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we define

\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}],\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}])

by means of

\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}:=\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}(e_{\mathbf{l}})^{\vee}\otimes e_{(m,\mathbf{l})}=\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes e_{(m,\mathbf{l})}.

(7.8)

Proposition 7.9
.

We have

\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}],\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}])=\mathbb{C}\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}.

Proof.

By definition, we have

\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}\colon\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}a_{\mathbf{l}}\,e_{\mathbf{l}}\,\longmapsto\,\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}a_{\mathbf{l}}\,e_{(m,\mathbf{l})}=e_{n}^{m}\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}a_{\mathbf{l}}\,e_{\mathbf{l}}.

Thus
\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)})=\mathbb{C}^{m}[e_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]
.
Since
M^{\prime}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{k}[e_{n}]
trivially, this shows that
\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}
respects
the
M^{\prime}
-action. Now the proposition follows from the fact that

\mathbb{C}^{m}[e_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]
has multiplicity-one in
\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}]
.
∎

We put

\mu^{\prime}:=1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu:=1+\tfrac{m+\ell}{n}.

As in (
5.4
),
we define

M
-representation
\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}]_{\alpha}

and

M^{\prime}
-representations
\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]_{\alpha}

for
\alpha\in\{\pm\}

as

\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]_{\alpha}=\mathbb{C}_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}]_{\alpha}=\mathbb{C}_{\alpha}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}].

Proposition 7.10
.

We have

\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]_{\alpha}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu^{\prime}},\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}]_{\alpha}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu})=\mathbb{C}\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}.

Proof.

By Proposition
7.9
, it suffices to show

\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]_{\alpha}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu^{\prime}},\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}]_{\alpha}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu}).

As
\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)})=\mathbb{C}^{m}[e_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]

and
\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}
respects the
M^{\prime}
-action,
it is further enough to show

\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu^{\prime}}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{m}[e_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu}

as
A^{\prime}
-modules.
Since
A^{\prime}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu^{\prime}}
by a character
with weight
-\mu^{\prime}
, one wishes to show that
A^{\prime}
acts on

\mathbb{C}^{m}[e_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu}
also by
-\mu^{\prime}
.

Observe that
the action of
M^{\prime}A^{\prime}
on
\mathbb{C}^{m}[e_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu}

comes from the restriction of that of
MA
on
\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu}
.
Recall from Remark
3.7
that
a^{\prime}
can be decomposed as

a^{\prime}=a^{\prime}_{M}a^{\prime}_{A}
with
a^{\prime}_{M}\in M
and
a^{\prime}_{A}\in A
. For

a^{\prime}=\mathrm{diag}(t,t^{\frac{-1}{n-1}},\ldots,t^{\frac{-1}{n-1}},1)\in A^{\prime},

we have

\displaystyle a^{\prime}_{M}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{diag}(1,t^{\frac{-1}{n(n-1)}},\ldots,t^{\frac{-1}{n(n-1)}},t^{\frac{1}{n}})\in M,

\displaystyle a^{\prime}_{A}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{diag}(t,t^{\frac{-1}{n}},\ldots,t^{\frac{-1}{n}},t^{\frac{-1}{n}})\in A.

Thus, for
e_{n}^{m}p(e^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{1}_{-\mu}\in\mathbb{C}^{m}[e_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu}
, we have

\displaystyle a^{\prime}\cdot(e_{n}^{m}p(e^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{1}_{-\mu})

\displaystyle=(a^{\prime}_{M}\cdot e_{n}^{m})(a^{\prime}_{M}\cdot p(e^{\prime}))\otimes(a^{\prime}_{A}\cdot\mathbb{1}_{-\mu})

\displaystyle=t^{\tfrac{m}{n}}\cdot t^{\tfrac{-\ell}{n(n-1)}}\cdot t^{-(1+\tfrac{m+\ell}{n})}(e_{n}^{m}p(e^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{1}_{-\mu})

\displaystyle=t^{-(1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1})}(e_{n}^{m}p(e^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{1}_{-\mu}).

Therefore,
A^{\prime}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{m}[e_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu}
also
by
-\mu^{\prime}=-(1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1})
.
Now the proposition follows.
∎

Now we define

\widetilde{\mathrm{Emb}}_{(m,\ell)}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]_{\alpha}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu^{\prime}},\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{n}}_{-})\otimes(\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}]_{\alpha}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu}))

as a map

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{Emb}}_{(m,\ell)}\colon p(e^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{1}_{\mu^{\prime}}\longmapsto 1\otimes\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}(p(e^{\prime}))\otimes\mathbb{1}_{\mu}.

We inflate the
MA
-representation

\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}]_{\alpha}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu}

to a
P
-representation by letting
N_{+}
act trivially.
Similarly, we inflate the
M^{\prime}A^{\prime}
-representation

\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]_{\alpha}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu^{\prime}}

to a
P^{\prime}
-representation.
Then, as
P^{\prime}\subset P
, we have

\widetilde{\mathrm{Emb}}_{(m,\ell)}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{P^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]_{\alpha}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\mu^{\prime}},M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{sym}^{m+\ell}_{n},-\mu)^{\alpha}).

Equivalently,
by applying to
\widetilde{\mathrm{Emb}}_{(m,\ell)}
the same convention
as (
4.25
) and (
7.7
), we have

\widetilde{\mathrm{Emb}}_{(m,\ell)}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},P^{\prime}}(M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1},-\mu^{\prime})^{\alpha},M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{sym}^{m+\ell}_{n},-\mu)^{\alpha}).

As a consequence, we obtain the following.

Proposition 7.11
.

We have

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},P^{\prime}}(M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1},-\mu^{\prime})^{\alpha},M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{sym}^{m+\ell}_{n},-\mu)^{\alpha})=\mathbb{C}\widetilde{\mathrm{Emb}}_{(m,\ell)}.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Proposition
7.10

and the preceding arguments.
∎

Now we are ready to show another factorization identity of
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}
.

Theorem 7.12
.

Let
n\geq 2
.
For
(\alpha,\beta;\sigma;s,r)\in\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},P^{\prime}),2}
,
the
({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},P^{\prime})
-homomorphism
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}
can be factored as follows:

\Phi_{(m,\ell)}=\Phi_{(m,0)}\circ\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell}=\varphi_{m+\ell}\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{Emb}}_{(m,\ell)}.

(7.13)

Equivalently, the following diagram commutes.

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 70.40854pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-44.18738pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 40.07927pt\raise-9.3978pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 74.01955pt\raise 6.68611pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.09723pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Phi_{(m,0)}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 126.6136pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 83.40854pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 126.6136pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},(m+\ell)-1)^{\alpha}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-18.7956pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 83.40854pt\raise-18.7956pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 174.22105pt\raise-18.7956pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-70.40854pt\raise-37.59119pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1},-(1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}))^{\alpha+m+\ell}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.74588pt\raise-18.7956pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.21725pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\ell}^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-9.26779pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.30429pt\raise-45.5773pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.23611pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{Emb}}_{(m,\ell)}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 102.40854pt\raise-37.59119pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 71.16597pt\raise-12.10948pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.09723pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Phi_{(m,\ell)}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 137.53792pt\raise-8.41664pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 83.40854pt\raise-37.59119pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 102.40854pt\raise-37.59119pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{sym}^{m+\ell}_{n},-(1+\tfrac{m+\ell}{n}))^{\alpha+m+\ell}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 177.22105pt\raise-18.7956pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.55972pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi_{m+\ell}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 177.22105pt\raise-8.41664pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 128.6898pt\raise-18.7956pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.70752pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circlearrowleft}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(7.14)

Proof.

Since any element in

M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1},-(1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}))^{\alpha+m+\ell}
is of the form

\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi^{\prime}_{\ell}}u_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes e_{\mathbf{l}}

for
u_{\mathbf{l}}\in\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{g}})
and
e_{\mathbf{l}}\in S^{\ell}(\mathbb{C}^{n-1})
,
it suffices to show the identities (
7.13
) for

u_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes e_{\mathbf{l}}
for
\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}
.

Recall from (
4.22
) that
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}
is given by

\Phi_{(m,\ell)}=(N_{n}^{-})^{m}\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}N_{\mathbf{l}}^{-}\otimes(e_{\mathbf{l}})^{\vee}=(N_{n}^{-})^{m}\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}N_{\mathbf{l}}^{-}\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}.

Then, by (
4.25
), we have

\Phi_{(m,\ell)}(u_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes e_{\mathbf{l}})=u_{\mathbf{l}}\Phi_{(m,\ell)}(e_{\mathbf{l}})=u_{\mathbf{l}}(N^{-}_{n})^{m}N^{-}_{\mathbf{l}}.

By (
7.5
) and (
7.7
),
the element

(\Phi_{(m,0)}\circ\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell})(u_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes e_{\mathbf{l}})
is given by

\displaystyle(\Phi_{(m,0)}\circ\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell})(u_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes e_{\mathbf{l}})

\displaystyle=u_{\mathbf{l}}(\Phi_{(m,0)}\circ\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell})(e_{\mathbf{l}})

\displaystyle=u_{\mathbf{l}}\Phi_{(m,0)}(N^{-}_{\mathbf{l}})

\displaystyle=u_{\mathbf{l}}(N^{-}_{n})^{m}N^{-}_{\mathbf{l}}.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle(\varphi_{m+\ell}\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{Emb}}_{(m,\ell)})(u_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes e_{\mathbf{l}})

\displaystyle=u_{\mathbf{l}}(\varphi_{m+\ell}\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{Emb}}_{(m,\ell)})(e_{\mathbf{l}})

\displaystyle=u_{\mathbf{l}}\varphi_{m+\ell}(1\otimes e^{m}_{n}e_{\mathbf{l}})

\displaystyle=u_{\mathbf{l}}(N^{-}_{n})^{m}N^{-}_{\mathbf{l}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

.

It follows from (
3.18
) that if
n=2
, then the factorization
identity (
7.14
) becomes

Remark 7.16
.

In Section
9
, we shall discuss the commutative diagram (
7.14
)
from an aspect of the branching laws of generalized Verma modules
(see Remark
9.37
).

7.3.
Factorization identities for
\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}

Now we consider the factorization identities of the differential symmetry breaking
operator
\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}
. Recall that

\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes e_{(m,\mathbf{l})}
may be identified with

\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes e_{(m,\mathbf{l})}=\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes(\widetilde{y}_{(m,\mathbf{l})})^{\vee}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[y^{\prime},y_{n}],\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[y^{\prime}]),

(7.17)

where
\widetilde{y}_{(m,\mathbf{l})}=y_{n}^{m}\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}
.
Also, recall that, by the duality theorem (Theorem
2.3
), we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},P^{\prime}}(M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1},-\mu^{\prime})^{\alpha},M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{sym}^{m+\ell}_{n},-\mu)^{\alpha})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathrm{Diff}_{G^{\prime}}(I(\mathrm{poly}^{m+\ell}_{n},\mu)^{\alpha},J(\mathrm{poly}^{\ell}_{n-1},\mu^{\prime})^{\alpha}).

Write

\widetilde{\mathrm{Proj}}_{(m,\ell)}=\mathcal{D}_{H\to D}(\widetilde{\mathrm{Emb}}_{(m,\ell)}).

Via the identification (
7.17
), we have

\widetilde{\mathrm{Proj}}_{(m,\ell)}=\mathrm{Rest}_{x_{n}=0}\circ\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi^{\prime}_{\ell}}\mathrm{id}\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes e_{(m,\mathbf{l})}.

That is, for
F(x^{\prime},x_{n})\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[y^{\prime}]
with

\displaystyle F(x^{\prime},x_{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\Xi_{m+\ell}}f_{\mathbf{k}}(x^{\prime},x_{n})\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{k}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{m+\ell}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in\Xi_{r}^{\prime}}f_{(m+\ell-r,\mathbf{r})}(x^{\prime},x_{n})\otimes\widetilde{y}_{(m+\ell-r,\mathbf{r})},

we have

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\mathrm{Proj}}_{(m,\ell)}F)(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}}f_{(m,\mathbf{l})}(x^{\prime},0)\otimes\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}.

The following is the differential-operator counterpart of Theorem
7.12
.

Theorem 7.18
.

Let
n\geq 2
. For

(\alpha,\beta;\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,2}
,
the differential symmetry breaking operator

\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}
can be factored as follows:

\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\ell}\circ\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}=\widetilde{\mathrm{Proj}}_{(m,\ell)}\circ\mathcal{D}_{m+\ell}.

Equivalently, the following diagram commutes.

(7.19)

Proof.

This simply follows from Theorem

Remark 7.20
.

As for Remark
7.15
,
it follows from (
3.17
) that if
n=2
, then the factorization
identity (
7.19
) is given as

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 63.90335pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-45.34355pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
