Remark 7.21
.

The commutative diagram (
7.19
) implies that we have

\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}|_{\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}_{m+\ell})}\colon\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}_{m+\ell})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\ell})

(see (
1.2
)).
In Proposition
8.9
below, we shall show that

\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}|_{\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}_{m+\ell})})=\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\ell})
.

8.
The
SL(n,\mathbb{R})
-representations on the image
\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D})

The aim of this section is to determine the image
\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D})
of the
differential symmetry breaking operators

\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)},\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}

in the commutative diagram (
7.19
)
for
(\alpha,\beta;\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,2}
,
where

\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,2}=\{(\alpha,\alpha+m+\ell;\mathrm{poly}_{n-1}^{\ell};1-(m+\ell),1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}):\alpha\in\{\pm\}\;\text{and}\;\ell,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}.

Here, by abuse of notation, the image
\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D})
is understood as the underlying
({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},K^{\prime})
-module.
These are achieved in Section
8.2
.

To make the argument simpler, we only consider the case
m,\ell\in 1+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
In this section we assume
n\geq 3
, unless otherwise specified.

8.1.
Preliminaries on
I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha}
and
J(\mathrm{triv},\nu)^{\beta}

We first recall from
[
20
]
necessary facts on the induced
representations
I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha}
of
G=SL(n+1,\mathbb{R})
and

J(\mathrm{triv},\nu)^{\beta}
of
G^{\prime}=SL(n,\mathbb{R})
.

Fact 8.1

(cf.
[
8
,
21
,
26
]
)
.

Let
n\geq 3
.
For
\beta\in\{\pm\}\equiv\{\pm 1\}
and
\nu\in\mathbb{C}
,
the induced representation
J(\mathrm{triv},\nu)^{\beta}
enjoys the following.

(1)

The induced representation
J(\mathrm{triv},\nu)^{\beta}
is irreducible except the following
two cases.

(A)

\nu\in-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
\beta=(-1)^{\nu}
.

(B)

\nu\in n+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
\beta=(-1)^{\nu+n}
.

(2)

For Case (A) with
\nu=-m
, there exists
a finite-dimensional irreducible subrepresentation

F_{G^{\prime}}(-m)^{\beta}\subset J(\mathrm{triv},-m)^{\beta}
such that

J(\mathrm{triv},-m)^{\beta}/F_{G^{\prime}}(-m)^{\beta}
is irreducible and
infinite-dimensional.

(3)

For Case (B) with
\nu=n+m
,
there exists an infinite-dimensional
irreducible subrepresentation

T_{G^{\prime}}(n+m)^{\beta}\subset J(\mathrm{triv},n+m)^{\beta}
such that

J(\mathrm{triv},n+m)^{\beta}/T_{G^{\prime}}(n+m)^{\beta}

is irreducible and finite-dimensional.

(4)

For
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
the following non-split exact sequences of Fréchet
G^{\prime}
-modules hold:

\displaystyle\{0\}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow F_{G^{\prime}}(-m)^{\beta}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow J(\mathrm{triv},-m)^{\beta}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow T_{G^{\prime}}(n+m)^{\beta}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\{0\},

\displaystyle\{0\}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow T_{G^{\prime}}(n+m)^{\beta}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow J(\mathrm{triv},n+m)^{\beta}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow F_{G^{\prime}}(-m)^{\beta}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\{0\}.

Theorem 8.2

(
[
20
, Thm. 6.5]
)
.

Let
n\geq 3
, For
\beta\in\{\pm\}\equiv\{\pm 1\}
and

k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
the kernel
\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\prime})
and image
\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\prime})
of

G^{\prime}
-intertwining differential operator

\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\prime}\colon J(\mathrm{triv},1-k)^{\beta}\longrightarrow J(\mathrm{poly}^{k}_{n-1},1+\tfrac{k}{n-1})^{\beta+k}

are given as follows.

(1)

k=0:
We have

\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}_{0}^{\prime})^{\beta}=\{0\}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{D}_{0}^{\prime})^{\beta}=J(\mathrm{triv},1)^{\beta},

(2)

k\in 1+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}:

We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\prime})^{\beta}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}F_{G^{\prime}}(1-k)^{\beta}&\text{if $\beta=(-1)^{1-k}$,}\\[3.0pt]
\{0\}&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\prime})^{\beta}

\displaystyle\simeq\begin{cases}T_{G^{\prime}}(n+k-1)^{\beta}&\text{if $\beta=(-1)^{1-k}$,}\\[3.0pt]
J(\mathrm{triv},1-k)^{\beta}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

In what follows, we simply write

F_{G^{\prime}}(-k)
,
T_{G^{\prime}}(n+k)
,
\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{k})
, and
\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{k})
.
Likewise,
we denote by
F_{G}(-k)
and
T_{G}(n+1+k)
the composition factors
of
I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha}
.

To end this subsection, we compute
the submodule
F_{G}(1-k)
of
I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha}
in the noncompact picture
of
I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha}
,
that is, the realization of
I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha}
as

I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha}\subset C^{\infty}(N_{-})\simeq C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Let
d\pi_{\lambda}
be the infinitesimal representation of

{\mathfrak{g}}={\mathfrak{n}}_{-}\oplus{\mathfrak{l}}\oplus{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}
on

I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha}
in the noncompact picture.
Then, as in (
2.9
),
for
f\in I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha}\subset C^{\infty}(N_{-})
,
we have

d\pi_{\lambda}(X)f(\bar{n})=\lambda d\chi((\mathrm{Ad}(\bar{n}^{-1})X)_{\mathfrak{l}})f(\bar{n})-\left(dR((\mathrm{Ad}(\cdot^{-1})X)_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}})f\right)(\bar{n}).

(8.3)

Here
d\chi
denotes the differential of the character
\chi
defined in
(
3.8
).
(For the details of (
8.3
), see, for instance,

[
20
, Sect. 2.2]
.)

The following lemma is used to compute
F_{G}(1-k)
.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
d\pi_{\lambda}
be the infinitesimal representation of

{\mathfrak{g}}={\mathfrak{n}}_{-}\oplus{\mathfrak{l}}\oplus{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}
on

I(\mathrm{triv},\lambda)^{\alpha}
in the noncompact picture.
Via the diffeomorphism (
4.13
),
the following hold.

(1)

For
N_{j}^{+}\in{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}
with
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, we have

d\pi_{\lambda}(N_{j}^{+})=x_{j}(\lambda+E_{x}),

where
E_{x}
denotes the Euler homogeneity operator for
x
. In particular,

d\pi_{\lambda}(N_{j}^{+})|_{\mathbb{C}^{a}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]}=(\lambda+a)x_{j}.

(2)

For
N_{j}^{-}\in{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}
with
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, we have

d\pi_{\lambda}(N_{j}^{-})=-dR(N_{j}^{-})=-\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}},

where
dR(N_{j}^{-})
denotes the infinitesimal right translation of
N_{j}^{-}
on

\mathbb{C}^{\infty}(N_{-})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

(3)

For
Z\in{\mathfrak{l}}
, we have

d\pi_{\lambda}(Z)|_{\mathbb{C}^{a}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]}\subset\mathbb{C}^{a}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}].

Proof.

Since
each case can be shown similarly by computing (
8.3
),
we only demonstrate a proof of Case (3) here, provided that Case (2) is proven.

Take
Z\in{\mathfrak{l}}
. Then, for
\bar{n}=\exp(\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}N_{j}^{-})
, we have

\mathrm{Ad}(\bar{n}^{-1})Z=\exp(\mathrm{ad}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}N_{j}^{-})Z)=Z-\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}[N_{j}^{-},Z].

Since
Z\in{\mathfrak{l}}
and
\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}[N_{j}^{-},Z]\in{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}
,
the value of
\lambda d\chi((\mathrm{Ad}(\bar{n}^{-1})Z)_{{\mathfrak{l}}})
is given by

\lambda d\chi((\mathrm{Ad}(\bar{n}^{-1})Z)_{{\mathfrak{l}}})=\lambda d\chi(Z)\in\mathbb{C}.

Next,
observe that , as
[N_{j}^{-},Z]\in{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}
, the bracket
[N_{j}^{-},Z]
is of the form

[N_{j}^{-},Z]=\sum_{r=1}^{n}a_{rj}N_{r}^{-}\quad\text{for some $a_{rj}\in\mathbb{C}$}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle-dR((\mathrm{Ad}(\bar{n}^{-1})Z)_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}dR([N_{j}^{-},Z])

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j,r=1}^{n}a_{rj}x_{j}dR(N_{r}^{-})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j,r=1}^{n}a_{rj}x_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{r}}.

We note that the formula for Case (2) is used from line two to line three.
Thus, we have

dR((\mathrm{Ad}(\bar{n}^{-1})Z)_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}})|_{\mathbb{C}^{a}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]}\subset\mathbb{C}^{a}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}].

Since
d\pi_{\lambda}(Z)=\lambda d\chi((\mathrm{Ad}(\bar{n}^{-1})Z)_{{\mathfrak{l}}})-dR((\mathrm{Ad}(\bar{n}^{-1})Z)_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}})
,
this completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 8.5
.

Let
k\in 1+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
In the noncompact picture that
I(\mathrm{triv},1-k)^{\alpha}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we have

F_{G}(1-k)=\bigoplus_{a=0}^{k-1}\mathbb{C}^{a}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}].

Proof.

Since
F_{G}(1-k)
is a finite-dimensional irreducible representation of
G
,
there exists a lowest weight vector
f_{0}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in F_{G}(1-k)
. As being
a lowest weight vector, we have

d\pi_{1-k}(N_{j}^{-})f_{0}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=0\quad\text{for all $j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$}.

By Lemma
8.4
(2), this is equivalent to

\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}f_{0}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=0\quad\text{for all $j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$},

which shows that
f_{0}
is a constant function.

Now observe that, by Lemma
8.4
(1), we have

d\pi_{1-k}(N_{j}^{+})|_{\mathbb{C}^{a}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]}=(1-k+a)x_{j}.

Thus, the space
\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})f_{0}\subset F_{G}(1-k)
is given by

\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})f_{0}=\bigoplus_{a=0}^{k-1}\mathbb{C}^{a}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}].

It follows from Lemma
8.4
that

\bigoplus_{a=0}^{k-1}\mathbb{C}^{a}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
is a non-zero
{\mathfrak{g}}
-submodule
of
F_{G}(1-k)
.
Now the irreducibility of
F_{G}(1-k)
concludes the proposition.
∎

8.2.
The image
\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D})

We now determine the
SL(n,\mathbb{R})
-representations on the image
\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D})

of
\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)},\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}
.
Throughout this subsection we assume that

(\alpha,\beta;\varpi;\lambda,\nu)\in\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{SL,2}
; in particular,
the following commutative diagram holds.

\textstyle{I(\mathrm{triv},1-(m+\ell))^{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{D}_{m+\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}}
\textstyle{J(\mathrm{triv},1-\ell)^{\alpha+m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\circlearrowleft}
\textstyle{I(\mathrm{poly}^{m+\ell}_{n},1+\tfrac{m+\ell}{n})^{\alpha+m+\ell}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{Proj}}_{(m,\ell)}}
\scriptstyle{\circlearrowleft}
\textstyle{J(\mathrm{poly}^{\ell}_{n-1},1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1})^{\alpha+m+\ell}}

Proposition 8.6
.

We have

\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)})=J(\mathrm{triv},1-\ell)^{\alpha+m}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
J(\mathrm{triv},1-\ell)^{\alpha+m}

is reducible. By Fact
8.1
, the only possibilities of
\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)})
are

\{0\}
,
F_{G^{\prime}}(1-\ell)
, or
J(\mathrm{triv},1-\ell)^{\alpha+m}
.
It follows from Theorem
8.2
that
F_{G^{\prime}}(1-\ell)=\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}_{\ell}^{\prime})
.
Therefore, if
\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)})\in\{\{0\},F_{G^{\prime}}(1-\ell)\}
, then

\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}=\mathcal{D}_{\ell}^{\prime}\circ\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}\equiv 0,

which contradicts the fact that
\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}\not\equiv 0
.
Hence,
\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)})=J(\mathrm{triv},1-\ell)^{\alpha+m}
.
∎

Proposition 8.7
.

The following hold.

(1)

Suppose
\alpha=(-1)^{m}
. Then we have

\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)})=\begin{cases}T_{G^{\prime}}(n+\ell-1)&\text{if $\ell\in 1+2\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$},\\[3.0pt]
J(\mathrm{triv},1-\ell)&\text{if $\ell\in 2(1+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})$}.\end{cases}

(2)

Suppose
\alpha=(-1)^{m+1}
. Then we have

\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)})=\begin{cases}J(\mathrm{triv},1-\ell)&\text{if $\ell\in 1+2\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$},\\[3.0pt]
T_{G^{\prime}}(n+\ell+1)&\text{if $\ell\in 2(1+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})$}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}=\mathcal{D}_{\ell}^{\prime}\circ\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}
,
it follows from Proposition
8.6
that

\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)})=\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\ell})
. Now the proposition follows from
Theorem
8.2
.
∎

8.3.
The image
\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}|_{F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))})

We next consider the image
\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}|_{F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))})

of the restricted operator
\mathbb{D}|_{F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))}

for
\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)},\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}
.

Proposition 8.8
.

Suppose that
I(\mathrm{triv},1-(m+\ell))^{\alpha}
is reducible. Then we have

\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}|_{F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))})=\{0\}.

Proof.

It follows from Theorem
8.2
that

F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))=\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}_{m+\ell})
. Therefore,

\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}|_{F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))}=(\widetilde{\mathrm{Proj}}_{(m,\ell)}\circ\mathcal{D}_{m+\ell})|_{\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}_{m+\ell})}\equiv 0.

∎

Suppose that
I(\mathrm{triv},1-(m+\ell))^{\alpha}
is reducible.
Then Fact
8.1
shows that
\alpha
satisfies the condition

\alpha=(-1)^{1-(m+\ell)}
, which is equivalent to

\alpha\cdot(-1)^{m}=(-1)^{1-\ell}
.
By Fact
8.1
, this implies that
J(\mathrm{triv},1-\ell)^{\alpha+m}
is also reducible;
in particular, in this case, we have

\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}_{\ell}^{\prime})=F_{G^{\prime}}(1-\ell)
. This observation is used in the proof of the
following proposition.

Proposition 8.9
.

Suppose that
I(\mathrm{triv},1-(m+\ell))^{\alpha}
is reducible. Then we have

\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}|_{F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))})=F_{G^{\prime}}(1-\ell)^{\alpha+m}.

Proof.

It follows from Theorem
7.18
that

\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\ell}\circ\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}
.
Thus, by Proposition
8.8
, we have

(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\ell}\circ\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)})|_{F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))}=\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}|_{F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))}\equiv 0,

which implies that

\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}|_{F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))})\subset\mathrm{Ker}(\mathcal{D}_{\ell}^{\prime})=F_{G^{\prime}}(1-\ell).

The irreducibility of
F_{G^{\prime}}(1-\ell)
forces that

\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}|_{F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))})=\{0\}
or
F_{G^{\prime}}(1-\ell)
.

It follows from Proposition
8.5
that

F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))
is given by

F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))=\bigoplus_{a=0}^{(m+\ell)-1}\mathbb{C}^{a}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}].

In particular, we have
x^{m}_{n}\in F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))
.
As
\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}=\mathrm{Rest}_{x_{n}=0}\circ\tfrac{\partial^{m}}{\partial x_{n}^{m}}
,
the function
\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}x_{n}^{m}
is given by

\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}x_{n}^{m}=m!\neq 0.

Thus,
\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}|_{F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))})\neq\{0\}
.
Consequently, we have

\mathrm{Im}(\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}|_{F_{G}(1-(m+\ell))})=F_{G^{\prime}}(1-\ell).

∎

9.
Branching laws of generalized Verma modules

The aim of this section is to discuss the branching law

M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},s)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}
of a generalized Verma module

M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},s)
of scalar-type. In addition, we also consider
the branching law of the image

\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})
of the
{\mathfrak{g}}
-homomorphism

\varphi_{p+1}\colon M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{sym}_{n}^{p+1},-(1+\tfrac{p+1}{n}))\to M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},p)

(9.1)

for
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
where
\varphi_{p+1}
is defined as in (
7.5
).
The branching laws are achieved in Theorems
9.22

and
9.27
.
In this section we assume
n\geq 2
, unless otherwise specified.

9.1.
Kobayashi’s character identity

To compute the branching law of a generalized Verma module,
the decomposition of the formal character is useful.
A key tool for it is Kobayashi’s character identity
[
11
]
.
We then start this section by recalling from
[
11
]

the character formula in a general framework.

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
be a complex simple Lie algebra. Choose a Cartan subalgebra
{\mathfrak{h}}
and
write
\Delta\equiv\Delta({\mathfrak{g}},{\mathfrak{h}})
for the set of roots of
{\mathfrak{g}}
with respect to
{\mathfrak{h}}
.
Fix a positive system
\Delta^{+}
and denote by

{\mathfrak{b}}
the Borel subalgebra of
{\mathfrak{g}}
associated with
\Delta^{+}
, namely,

{\mathfrak{b}}={\mathfrak{h}}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}_{+}
with
{\mathfrak{u}}_{+}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Delta^{+}}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\alpha}
.
Here
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\alpha}
is the root space
for
\alpha\in\Delta^{+}
. Let
\mathcal{O}
denote the BGG category of
{\mathfrak{g}}
-modules
whose objects are finitely generated
{\mathfrak{g}}
-modules that are
{\mathfrak{h}}
-semisimple
and locally
{\mathfrak{u}}_{+}
-finite.

Let
{\mathfrak{p}}\supset{\mathfrak{b}}
be a standard parabolic subalgebra of
{\mathfrak{g}}
. Write

{\mathfrak{p}}={\mathfrak{l}}\oplus{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}
for the Levi decomposition of
{\mathfrak{p}}
with
{\mathfrak{h}}\subset{\mathfrak{l}}
.
We put
\Delta^{+}({\mathfrak{l}}):=\{\alpha\in\Delta^{+}:{\mathfrak{g}}_{\alpha}\subset{\mathfrak{l}}\}
.
We denote by
\mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{p}}
the parabolic BGG category, which is a full
subcategory of
\mathcal{O}
whose objects are
{\mathfrak{l}}
-semisimple and locally

{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}
-finite.

Let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denote the inner product on
{\mathfrak{h}}^{*}
induced from
a non-degenerate symmetric bilinear form
of
{\mathfrak{g}}
. For
\alpha\in\Delta
, we write
\alpha^{\vee}=2\alpha/\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle
.
Then we put

\Lambda^{+}({\mathfrak{l}}):=\{\lambda\in{\mathfrak{h}}^{*}:\langle\lambda,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\;\;\text{for all $\alpha\in\Delta^{+}({\mathfrak{l}})$\lx@text@rbrace}.

For
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}({\mathfrak{l}})
, we denote by
F_{\lambda}
the finite-dimensional
simple
{\mathfrak{g}}
-module with highest weight
\lambda
. By letting
{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}
act trivially,
we regard
F_{\lambda}
as a
{\mathfrak{p}}
-module. We then define the generalized Verma
module
M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\lambda)
with highest weight
\lambda
by

M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\lambda)=\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{g}})\otimes_{\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{p}})}F_{\lambda}.

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
be a reductive subalgebra of
{\mathfrak{g}}
and
take a hyperbolic element
H\in{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
, that is,
the eigenvalues of
\mathrm{ad}(H)
on
{\mathfrak{g}}

are all real-valued. We then define the subalgebras

{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}(H),\quad{\mathfrak{l}}(H),\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}(H)

as the sum of the eigenspaces of negative, zero, and positive
eiganvalue of
\mathrm{ad}(H)
, respectively, so that we have

{\mathfrak{g}}={\mathfrak{n}}_{-}(H)\oplus{\mathfrak{l}}(H)\oplus{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}(H)
.

Now suppose that
{\mathfrak{p}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
is
a
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
-compatible
parabolic subalgebra
determined by a hyperbolic element
H\in{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
,
namely,
{\mathfrak{p}}={\mathfrak{p}}(H):={\mathfrak{l}}(H)\oplus{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}(H)
.
The
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
-compatibility of
{\mathfrak{p}}
implies that

{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}:={\mathfrak{p}}\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
is a parabolic subalgebra of
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}

with Levi decomposition

{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}={\mathfrak{l}}^{\prime}\oplus{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}^{\prime}:=({\mathfrak{l}}(H)\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime})\oplus({\mathfrak{n}}_{+}(H)\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}).

We choose a Cartan subalgebra

{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
in such a way that
H\in{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}
and that
it extends to a Cartan subalgebra
{\mathfrak{h}}
of
{\mathfrak{g}}
. Then we have

{\mathfrak{h}}\subset{\mathfrak{l}}(H)

and
{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
. Hereafter, we simply write

{\mathfrak{l}}={\mathfrak{l}}(H)
and
{\mathfrak{n}}_{\pm}={\mathfrak{n}}_{\pm}(H)
.

Given a finite-dimensional vector space
V
, we write

S(V)=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\infty}S^{k}(V)
for the symmetric tensor algebra
of
V
.
For finite-dimensional simple
{\mathfrak{g}}
- and
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
-modules

F_{\lambda}
and
F^{\prime}_{\delta}
with highest weights
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}({\mathfrak{l}})

and
\delta\in\Lambda^{+}({\mathfrak{l}}^{\prime})
, respectively, we set

m(\delta;\lambda):=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{\prime}}(F^{\prime}_{\delta},F_{\lambda}|_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{\prime}}\otimes S({\mathfrak{n}}_{-}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime})).

Let
[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\lambda)]
and
[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\delta)]
denote the formal characters
of
M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\lambda)
and
M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\delta)
, respectively.

Theorem 9.2

(
[
11
, Thm. 3.10]
)
.

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{p}}={\mathfrak{l}}\oplus{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}
is a
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
-compatible parabolic subalgebra
of
{\mathfrak{g}}
. Then, for
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}({\mathfrak{l}})
, the following hold.

(1)

m(\delta;\lambda)<\infty
for all
\delta\in\Lambda^{+}({\mathfrak{l}}^{\prime})
.

(2)

In the Grothendieck group of
\mathcal{O}^{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}
, we have

[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\lambda)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]\simeq\bigoplus_{\delta\in\mathrm{supp}(\lambda)}m(\delta;\lambda)[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\delta)],

(9.3)

where
\mathrm{supp}(\lambda)=\{\delta\in\Lambda^{+}({\mathfrak{l}}^{\prime}):m(\delta;\lambda)\neq 0\}
.

In what follows, we resume the notation and normalizations
specified in Section
3
. So, we have

{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathfrak{sl}(n+1,\mathbb{C})
and
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}\simeq\mathfrak{sl}(n,\mathbb{C})

realized as in (
3.1
).
We remark that the maximal parabolic subalgebra

{\mathfrak{p}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
in consideration is not
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
-compatible, as
the defining hyperbolic element
H_{0}\in{\mathfrak{h}}
in (
3.2
) is not in
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
.
However, as
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}={\mathfrak{p}}\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
with

{\mathfrak{l}}^{\prime}={\mathfrak{l}}\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}^{\prime}={\mathfrak{n}}_{+}\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
,
a careful observation on the proof of Theorem
9.2

shows that the character identity (
9.3
) holds also in the present case.
In the next subsection, we apply the character identity (
9.3
) to
show the branching law
[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},s)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]
of the formal character
of a generalized Verma module
M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},s)
.

9.2.
Branching law of
[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]

For brevity, we simply write

M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)=M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},s)\quad\text{and}\quad M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(r)=M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{triv},r).

Theorem 9.4
.

Let
n\geq 2
. For
s\in\mathbb{C}
,
the following isomorphism holds
in the Grothendieck group of
\mathcal{O}^{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}
:

[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]\simeq\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(s-m)].

(9.5)

Proof.

Observe that we have
{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}\simeq\mathbb{C}N_{n}^{-}
.
It thus follows from Lemma
5.5
that we have

S^{m}({\mathfrak{n}}_{-}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime})\simeq\mathbb{C}_{-m}
as
{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}
-modules.
Then the decomposition

(\mathbb{C}_{s}\otimes S({\mathfrak{n}}_{-}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}))|_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{\prime}}
is given as

\displaystyle(\mathbb{C}_{s}\otimes S({\mathfrak{n}}_{-}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}))|_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}(\mathbb{C}_{s}\otimes S^{m}({\mathfrak{n}}_{-}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{-}\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}))|_{{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathbb{C}_{s-m}.

Now the character identity (
9.3
) concludes (
9.5
).
∎

Corollary 9.6
.

For
s\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have

M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}\simeq\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(s-m).

(9.7)

Proof.

By the classification of the reducibility points
of generalized Verma modules of scalar type (cf.
[
1
,
6
,
7
]
),
if
s\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, then
M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(s-m)
is
a simple
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
-module for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
Now the proposed assertion follows from
Theorem
9.4
.
∎

In Section
9.4
below,
we shall show that the isomorphism (
9.7
) indeed holds for any
s\in\mathbb{C}

by making use of the classification of
the
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\prime}_{+}
-invariant subspaces of
M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)
.

9.3.
Branching law of
[\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]

Now we consider
the branching law of the formal character of
the image
\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})
of the
{\mathfrak{g}}
-homomorphism

\varphi_{p+1}
in (
7.5
).

We first recall from
[
20
]
the classification of

{\mathfrak{g}}
-homomorphisms between generalized Verma modules in consideration.
Define
\Lambda^{n+1}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\subset\mathrm{Irr}(\mathfrak{sl}(n,\mathbb{C}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
as

\Lambda^{n+1}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}:=\{(\mathrm{sym}_{n}^{k};k-1,-(1+\tfrac{k}{n})):k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}.

Theorem 9.8

(
[
20
, Thm. 5.23]
)
.

We have

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}(M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\tau,u),M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},s))=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{id}&\text{if $(\tau,u)=(\mathrm{triv},s)$,}\\
\mathbb{C}\varphi_{k}&\text{if $(\tau;s,u)\in\Lambda_{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{n+1}$,}\\
\{0\}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Now, let
s=p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
By (
7.5
), the image

\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})\subset M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)=M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathrm{triv},p)

is

\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})=\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{g}})\left(\mathbb{C}^{p+1}[N_{1}^{-},\ldots,N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}\right).

Then, as
{\mathfrak{g}}
-modules, we have

M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)/\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})\simeq S^{p}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1}).

Since
(\mathrm{sym}^{p}_{n+1},S^{p}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1}))

is a simple
{\mathfrak{g}}
-module,
this implies that
\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})

is a unique maximal submodule of
M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)
.
The formal character
[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)]
then satisfies

[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)]\simeq[\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})]+[S^{p}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1})].

(9.9)

As in Section
7
, we write
\varphi_{k}^{\prime}
for

{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
-homomorphisms between generalized Verma modules of
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
.
Then, for
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have

[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(d)]\simeq[\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1})]+[S^{d}(\mathbb{C}^{n})].

(9.10)

Now we are ready to show the branching law of

\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})
in the Grothendiek group of
\mathcal{O}^{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}
.

Theorem 9.11
.

Let
n\geq 2
. For
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
the following isomorphism holds
in the Grothendieck group of
\mathcal{O}^{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}
:

[\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]\simeq\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}[\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{d+1}^{\prime})]\oplus\bigoplus_{j\geq 1}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j)].

(9.12)

Further, for
n=2
, we have

[\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]\simeq\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}2\cdot[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-(d+2))]\oplus[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-1)]\oplus\bigoplus_{j\geq p+3}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j)].

(9.13)

Proof.

We first show the isomorphism (
9.12
).
We consider the branching law
[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]
in two ways.
First, by (
9.5
), we have

\displaystyle[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(p-m)]

(9.14)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(d)]\oplus\bigoplus_{j\geq 1}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j)]

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}[\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1})]\oplus[S^{p}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]\oplus\bigoplus_{j\geq 1}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j)].

(9.15)

We note that
the character identity
(
9.10
) for
[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(d)]
and the classical branching law

S^{p}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}\simeq\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}S^{d}(\mathbb{C}^{n})

are applied from line two to line three.

On the other hand, by (
9.9
), the formal character

[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]
also satisfies

[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]\simeq[\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]\oplus[S^{p}(\mathbb{C}^{n+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}].

(9.16)

By comparing (
9.15
) with (
9.16
), we obtain

[\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}]\simeq\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}[\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1})]\oplus\bigoplus_{j\geq 1}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j)].

Now, to show (
9.13
), let
n=2
. In this case,
the map
\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1}
is a
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
-homomorphism

\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1}\colon M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-(d+2))\longrightarrow M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(d).

Since
M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-(d+2))
is of scalar type,

\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1}
is injective for all
d\in\{0,\ldots,p\}

(cf.
[
9
, Prop. 9.11]
).
Thus, the image
\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1})
is given by

\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1})\simeq M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-(d+2))
, which shows that

\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}[\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1})]\simeq\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-(d+2))].

(9.17)

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j\geq 1}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j)]

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{j=2}^{p+2}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j)]\oplus[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-1)]\oplus\bigoplus_{j\geq p+3}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j)]

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-(2+d))]\oplus[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-1)]\oplus\bigoplus_{j\geq p+3}[M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j)].

(9.18)

Now (
9.13
) follows from (
9.12
),
(
9.17
), and (
9.18
).
∎

In the next section, we show that
the actual branching law
\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}

is indeed given as in
(
9.12
) and (
9.13
).

9.4.
Branching laws of
M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}
and

\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}

Now we show the actual branching laws of

M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}
and
\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}
.
Our idea is to observe
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\prime}_{+}
-subspaces of

M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)
and
\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})
.
In particular, the rest of the arguments is nothing to do with the character
identity (
9.3
).

It follows from
Propositions
5.18
and
6.6

and the algebraic Fourier transform
F_{c}
in (
2.11
) that

\mathbb{C}^{m}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{s}\subset M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)^{{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}^{\prime}}\quad\text{for all $m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$}.

(9.19)

As
{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}
-modules, we have

\mathbb{C}^{m}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{s}\simeq\mathbb{C}_{s-m}\quad\text{for all $m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$},

(9.20)

which yields an isomorphism

\mathbb{C}[N_{1}^{-},\ldots,N_{n-1}^{-}]\mathbb{C}^{m}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{s}=\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime})(\mathbb{C}^{m}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{s})\simeq M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(s-m).

(9.21)

Theorem 9.22
.

Let
n\geq 2
. For any
s\in\mathbb{C}
, we have

M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}\simeq\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(s-m).

(9.23)

Proof.

By (
9.21
), we have

\displaystyle M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{g}})\otimes_{\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{p}})}\mathbb{C}_{s}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[N_{1}^{-},\ldots,N_{n-1}^{-},N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{s}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathbb{C}[N_{1}^{-},\ldots,N_{n-1}^{-}]\mathbb{C}^{m}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{s}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(s-m).

∎

In what follows, we write

\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]=\mathbb{C}[N_{1}^{-},\ldots,N_{n-1}^{-}]\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]=\mathbb{C}[N_{1}^{-},\ldots,N_{n-1}^{-},N_{n}^{-}].

Then, by (
7.5
),
the images

\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})\subset M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)
for
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}

and

\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1})\subset M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(d)
for
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}

are given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{g}})\left(\mathbb{C}^{p+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}\right),

(9.24)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime})\left(\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d}\right),

(9.25)

where we have

\mathbb{C}^{p+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}\subset M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)^{{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d}\subset M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(d)^{{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}^{\prime}}.

(9.26)

Theorem 9.27
.

Let
n\geq 2
. For
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have

\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}\simeq\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{d+1}^{\prime})\oplus\bigoplus_{j\geq 1}M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j).

(9.28)

In partucular, for
n=2
, we have

\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}\simeq\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}2\cdot M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-(d+2))\oplus M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-1)\oplus\bigoplus_{j\geq p+3}M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j).

(9.29)

Proof.

Since (
9.29
) follows from (
9.28
)
as in Theorem
9.11
, it suffices to show (
9.28
).

First observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}^{p+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b=0}^{p+1}\mathbb{C}^{b}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{p+1-b}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}

\displaystyle\simeq\sum_{b=0}^{p+1}\mathbb{C}^{b}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{b-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{d=0}^{p}\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d}+(1\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-1}).

(9.30)

We note that (
9.20
) is applied from line one to line two.
Then, by (
9.24
) and (
9.30
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{g}})\left(\mathbb{C}^{p+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\mathbb{C}^{p+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}

\displaystyle=\sum_{d=0}^{p}\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d}+\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-1}

(9.31)

\displaystyle=:\text{(B)}+\text{(A)}.

(9.32)

The second term
(A)
is given as

\displaystyle\text{(A)}=\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-1}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{c=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{c}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-1}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{c=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-1-c}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-j}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{\infty}M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j).

(9.33)

By (
9.19
), we have

\mathbb{C}_{-j}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{c}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-1}\subset M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(-1)^{{\mathfrak{n}}_{+}^{\prime}}
,
which verifies the identity from line three to line four.

Next, for
(B)
, we have

(B)

\displaystyle=\sum_{d=0}^{p}\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d}

\displaystyle=\sum_{d=0}^{p}\sum_{c=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{c}[N_{n}^{-}]\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d}

\displaystyle=\sum_{d=0}^{p}\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d}+\sum_{d=0}^{p}\sum_{c=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{c}[N_{n}^{-}]\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d}

\displaystyle=:\text{(B1)}+\text{(B2)}.

(9.34)

By (
9.25
) and (
9.26
),
(B1)
is given by

\displaystyle\text{(B1)}=\sum_{d=0}^{p}\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}\mathcal{U}({\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime})\left(\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1}).

(9.35)

The actual realizations of
M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j)
in
(A)
and
\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1})
in
(B1)

imply that
the sum
\text{(B1)}+\text{(A)}
is indeed a direct sum

\text{(B1)}\oplus\text{(A)}
.
We then claim the following.

Claim 9.36
.

We have
\text{(B2)}\subset\text{(A)}\oplus\text{(B1)}
.

Suppose that Claim
9.36
holds. Then,
by (
9.32
), (
9.33
), (
9.34
), and (
9.35
),
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})=\text{(B)}+\text{(A)}

\displaystyle=\text{(B1)}+\text{(B2)}+\text{(A)}

\displaystyle=\text{(B1)}\oplus\text{(A)}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{d=0}^{p}\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1})\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{\infty}M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-j),

which is what we wish to show. Thus, in the rest of the proof, we aim to show
Claim
9.36
.

Write

\text{(C)}=\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{c}[N_{n}^{-}]\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d},

so that

\text{(B2)}=\sum_{d=0}^{p}\sum_{c=1}^{\infty}\text{(C)}
.

First observe that
(A)
,
(B)
, and
(C)
are given by

(A)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\mathbb{C}^{p+1}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p},

(B1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{d=0}^{p}\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b=1}^{p+1}\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{b}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{p+1-b}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p},

(C)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{c}[N_{n}^{-}]\mathbb{C}^{d+1}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{d}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{c}[N_{n}^{-}]\mathbb{C}^{b}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{p+1-b}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{b}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{p+1+c-b}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}.

Thus, if
c-b\geq 0
, then

(C)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{b}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{p+1+c-b}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}

\displaystyle\subset\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime},N_{n}^{-}]\mathbb{C}^{p+1}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}

\displaystyle=\text{(A)}.

If
c-b<0
, then
-(p+1)\leq c-b\leq-1
. Thus,
the number
p+1+c-b
is of the form

p+1+c-b=p+1-b_{0}

for some
b_{0}\in\{1,\ldots,p+1\}
. Therefore,

(C)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{b}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{p+1-b_{0}}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}

\displaystyle\subset\sum_{b=1}^{p+1}\mathbb{C}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{b}[(N^{-})^{\prime}]\mathbb{C}^{p+1-b}[N_{n}^{-}]\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}

\displaystyle=\text{(B1)}.

As
\text{(B2)}=\sum_{d=0}^{p}\sum_{c=1}^{\infty}\text{(C)}
, this shows the claim.
∎

Remark 9.37
.

Here are some remarks on Theorems
9.22
and
9.27
.

(1)

The inclusion
M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(s-m)\hookrightarrow M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(s)
is given by
the normal derivative
\Phi_{(m,0)}
as in Theorems
4.26

and
4.27
.

(2)

By (
9.23
) and (
9.28
),
for
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
d\in[0,p]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
we have

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.52592pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.69794pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.62689pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\subset}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.91667pt\raise-10.48558pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigcup}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 41.77289pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.11491pt\raise-10.48558pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigcup}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern-23.52592pt\raise-20.97116pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.62689pt\raise-20.97116pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\subset}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 35.52592pt\raise-20.97116pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(9.38)

The square (
9.38
) corresponds to
the factorization identity
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}=\Phi_{(m,0)}\circ\varphi_{\ell}^{\prime}

in (
7.14
).

(3)

Let
M_{j}
for
j=1,2
denote the two copies of
M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(-(2+d))

in (
9.29
) such that
M_{1}=\mathrm{Im}(\varphi^{\prime}_{d+1})\subset M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(d)
.
Then (
9.29
) shows that, for
n=2
, we have

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 37.4479pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-37.4479pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M^{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}(d)\oplus M_{2}\hskip 10.0pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.58788pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\subset}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.91667pt\raise-10.48112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigcup}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 52.91716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\;\;M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 70.0369pt\raise-10.48112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.50005pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bigcup}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern-23.27777pt\raise-20.96225pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M_{1}\oplus M_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.58788pt\raise-20.96225pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\subset}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 49.4479pt\raise-20.96225pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{p+1})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(9.39)

This corresponds to the multiplicity-two phenomenon in
Theorem
4.27
. Namely,
the inclusions

M_{1}\hookrightarrow M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)

and
M_{2}\hookrightarrow M^{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)

are related to
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
-homomorphisms
\Phi_{(m,\ell)}=\Phi_{(p-d,d+1)}
and

\Phi_{(m+2\ell,0)}=\Phi_{(p+d+2,0)}
, respectively.

10.
Differential symmetry breaking operators
\mathbb{D}
for
(GL(n+1,\mathbb{R}),GL(n,\mathbb{R}))

The aim of this section is to classify and construct differential symmetry breaking operators
\mathbb{D}
for the pair
(G,G^{\prime})=(GL(n+1,\mathbb{R}),GL(n,\mathbb{R}))
with
maximal parabolic subgroups
(P,P^{\prime})
such that
G/P\simeq\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^{n}
and
G^{\prime}/P^{\prime}\simeq\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
. We also discuss
G
-intertwining differential operators
\mathcal{D}

and the factorization identities of
\mathbb{D}
. Those results are achieved in Theorems

10.3
,
10.6
, and
10.7
.
In this section we assume
n\geq 2
, unless otherwise specified.

10.1.
Notation

We start by introducing some notation.
Let
G=GL(n+1,\mathbb{R})
with Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}(\mathbb{R})=\mathfrak{gl}(n+1,\mathbb{R})

for
n\geq 2
. Let
G^{\prime}
denote the closed subgroup of
G
defined by

G^{\prime}=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}g^{\prime}&\\
&1\\
\end{pmatrix}:g^{\prime}\in GL(n,\mathbb{R})\right\}\simeq GL(n,\mathbb{R})

with Lie algebra

{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}X^{\prime}&\\
&0\end{pmatrix}:X^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{gl}(n,\mathbb{R})\right\}\simeq\mathfrak{gl}(n,\mathbb{R}).

Let
P=\text{Stab}_{G}(\mathbb{R}(1,0,\ldots,0)^{t})
and
P^{\prime}=G^{\prime}\cap P
. Then
P
and
P^{\prime}
are
parabolic subgroups of
G
and
G^{\prime}
, respectively, such that

G/P\simeq\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^{n}
and
G^{\prime}/P^{\prime}\simeq\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
.
Let
M
and
M^{\prime}
denote the subgroups of
P
and
P^{\prime}
, respectively,
defined by

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\varepsilon&\\
&g\\
\end{pmatrix}:\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}\;\text{and}\;g\in SL^{\pm}(n,\mathbb{R})\right\}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\times SL^{\pm}(n,\mathbb{R}),

\displaystyle M^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\varepsilon&&\\
&g^{\prime}&\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}:\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}\;\text{and}\;g^{\prime}\in SL^{\pm}(n-1,\mathbb{R})\right\}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\times SL^{\pm}(n-1,\mathbb{R}).

We write

\displaystyle J_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}(\sum^{n+1}_{r=2}E_{r,r})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{diag}(0,1,1,\ldots,1,1),

\displaystyle J_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}(\sum^{n}_{r=2}E_{r,r})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}\mathrm{diag}(0,1,1,\ldots,1,0)

and put

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle:=\exp(\mathbb{R}\widetilde{H}_{0}),\quad A_{2}

\displaystyle:=\exp(\mathbb{R}J_{0}),

\displaystyle A_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\exp(\mathbb{R}\widetilde{H}_{0}^{\prime}),\quad A_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\exp(\mathbb{R}J_{0}^{\prime}),

where
\widetilde{H}_{0}
and
\widetilde{H}_{0}^{\prime}
are the diagonal matrices defined in
(
3.4
) and (
3.5
), respectively.
We then define
A
and
A^{\prime}
by

A=A_{1}A_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad A^{\prime}=A_{1}^{\prime}A_{2}^{\prime}.

Let
N_{+}
and
N_{+}^{\prime}
be the unipotent subgroups defined in Section
3.1
.
Then
P=MAN_{+}
and
P^{\prime}=M^{\prime}A^{\prime}N_{+}^{\prime}
are Langlands decompositions of
P

and
P^{\prime}
, respectively.

For
(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}),(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
,
we define one-dimensional representations

\mathbb{C}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}=(\chi^{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})},\mathbb{C})

of
A=\exp(\mathbb{R}\widetilde{H}_{0})\exp(\mathbb{R}J_{0})

and

\mathbb{C}_{(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}=((\chi^{\prime})^{(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})},\mathbb{C})
of

A^{\prime}=\exp(\mathbb{R}\widetilde{H}_{0}^{\prime})\exp(\mathbb{R}J_{0}^{\prime})
by

\displaystyle\chi^{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}

\displaystyle\colon\exp(t_{1}\widetilde{H}_{0})\exp(t_{2}J_{0})\longmapsto\exp(\lambda_{1}t_{1})\exp(\lambda_{2}t_{2}),

\displaystyle(\chi^{\prime})^{(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}

\displaystyle\colon\exp(t_{1}\widetilde{H}_{0}^{\prime})\exp(t_{2}J_{0}^{\prime})\longmapsto\exp(\nu_{1}t_{1})\exp(\nu_{2}t_{2}).

Then
\mathrm{Irr}(A)
and
\mathrm{Irr}(A^{\prime})
are given by

\mathrm{Irr}(A)=\{\mathbb{C}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}:\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{C}\}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{Irr}(A^{\prime})=\{\mathbb{C}_{(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}:\nu_{j}\in\mathbb{C}\}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2}.

For
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\in\{\pm\}^{2}
,
a one-dimensional representation
\mathbb{C}_{(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})}
of
M

is defined by

\begin{pmatrix}\varepsilon&\\
&g\\
\end{pmatrix}\longmapsto\mathrm{sgn}^{\alpha_{1}}(\varepsilon)\cdot\mathrm{sgn}^{\alpha_{2}}(\det(g)),

where, for
b\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}
,
\mathrm{sgn}^{\alpha}(b)
is defined as in
(
3.10
).
Then

\mathrm{Irr}(M)_{\mathrm{fin}}\simeq\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL^{\pm}(n,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}

and

\mathrm{Irr}(M^{\prime})_{\mathrm{fin}}\simeq\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL^{\pm}(n-1,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}

are given as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Irr}(M)_{\mathrm{fin}}

\displaystyle\simeq\{\mathbb{C}_{\alpha_{1}}\boxtimes(\mathbb{C}_{\alpha_{2}}\otimes\xi):(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\xi)\in\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Irr}(M^{\prime})_{\mathrm{fin}}

\displaystyle\simeq\{\mathbb{C}_{\beta_{1}}\boxtimes(\mathbb{C}_{\beta_{2}}\otimes\varpi):(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\varpi)\in\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n-1,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\}.

Since

\mathrm{Irr}(P)_{\mathrm{fin}}\simeq\mathrm{Irr}(M)_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathrm{Irr}(A)
, the set
\mathrm{Irr}(P)_{\mathrm{fin}}
can be
parametrized by

\mathrm{Irr}(P)_{\mathrm{fin}}\simeq\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}.

Similarly, we have

\mathrm{Irr}(P^{\prime})_{\mathrm{fin}}\simeq\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n-1,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}.

For

(\bm{\alpha},\xi,\bm{\lambda})\in\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}

with
\bm{\alpha}=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
and
\bm{\lambda}=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
,
we write

I(\xi;\bm{\lambda})^{\bm{\alpha}}=\mathrm{Ind}_{P}^{G}\left(\mathbb{C}_{\alpha_{1}}\boxtimes(\mathbb{C}_{\alpha_{2}}\otimes\xi)\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}\right)

for (unnormalized) parabolically induced representations of
G
.
Likewise, for

(\bm{\beta},\varpi,\bm{\nu})\in\{\pm\}^{2}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n-1,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}

with
\bm{\beta}=(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
and
\bm{\nu}=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
, we write

J(\varpi;\bm{\nu})^{\bm{\beta}}=\mathrm{Ind}_{P^{\prime}}^{G^{\prime}}\left(\mathbb{C}_{\beta_{1}}\boxtimes(\mathbb{C}_{\beta_{2}}\otimes\varpi)\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}\right).

In the next three subsections we shall state the main results
of differential symmetry breaking operators

\mathbb{D}
,
G
-intertwining differential operators
\mathcal{D}
, and factorization identities.
In Section
10.5
, we shall discuss the proofs of the statements.

10.2.
Differential symmetry breaking operators
\mathbb{D}
for
(GL(n+1,\mathbb{R}),GL(n,\mathbb{R}))

For
n\geq 2
, we define

\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{GL,j}\subset\{\pm\}^{4}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n-1,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{4}

for
j=1,2
as follows.

\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{GL,1}:=\{(\bm{\alpha};\bm{\beta};\varpi;\bm{\lambda};\bm{\nu}):\text{$(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta};\varpi;\bm{\lambda},\bm{\nu})$ satisfies \eqref{eqn:GL2}
below for $\alpha_{j}\in\{\pm\}$, $\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{C}$, and $m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$.}\}

\displaystyle(\bm{\alpha};\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2};\alpha_{1}+m;\alpha_{2})

\displaystyle\varpi

\displaystyle=\mathrm{triv}

(10.1)

\displaystyle(\bm{\lambda};\bm{\nu})

\displaystyle=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2};\lambda_{1}+m,\lambda_{2})

\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{GL,2}:=\{(\bm{\alpha};\bm{\beta};\varpi;\bm{\lambda};\bm{\nu}):\text{$(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta};\varpi;\bm{\lambda},\bm{\nu})$ satisfies \eqref{eqn:GL3} below
for $\alpha_{j}\in\{\pm\}$ and $\ell,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$.}\}

\displaystyle(\bm{\alpha};\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2};\alpha_{1}+(m+\ell);\alpha_{2})

\displaystyle\varpi

\displaystyle=\mathrm{poly}_{n-1}^{\ell}

(10.2)

\displaystyle(\bm{\lambda};\bm{\nu})

\displaystyle=(1-(m+\ell),\lambda_{2};1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1},\lambda_{2}-\tfrac{\ell}{n-1})

Further, we put

\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{GL}:=\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{GL,1}\cup\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{GL,2}.

Let
\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}\in\mathrm{Diff}_{\mathbb{C}}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{n-1}])
be the differential operator defined in (
4.14
).

Theorem 10.3
.

Let
n\geq 2
. Then we have

\mathrm{Diff}_{G^{\prime}}\big(I(\mathrm{triv};\bm{\lambda})^{\bm{\alpha}},J(\varpi;\bm{\nu})^{\bm{\beta}}\big)=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}&\text{if $(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta};\varpi;\bm{\lambda},\bm{\nu})\in\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{GL,1}$,}\\[3.0pt]
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}&\text{if $(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta};\varpi;\bm{\lambda},\bm{\nu})\in\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{GL,2}$,}\\
\{0\}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Remark 10.4
.

As opposed to the
SL
case, the multiplicity-one property holds even for
n=2
.

10.3.
Intertwining differential operators
\mathcal{D}
for
GL(n,\mathbb{R})

For
n\geq 2
,
define

\Lambda^{n+1}_{GL}\subset\{\pm\}^{4}\times\mathrm{Irr}(SL(n,\mathbb{R}))_{\mathrm{fin}}\times\mathbb{C}^{4}

as follows.

\Lambda^{n+1}_{GL}:=\{(\bm{\alpha};\bm{\gamma};\xi;\bm{\lambda};\bm{\tau}):\text{$(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\gamma};\xi;\bm{\lambda},\bm{\tau})$ satisfies \eqref{eqn:GL1} below
for $\alpha_{j}\in\{\pm\}$, $\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{C}$, and $k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$.}\}

\displaystyle(\bm{\alpha};\bm{\gamma})

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2};\alpha_{1}+k;\alpha_{2})

(10.5)

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle=\mathrm{poly}_{n}^{k}

\displaystyle(\bm{\lambda};\bm{\tau})

\displaystyle=(1-k,\lambda_{2};1+\tfrac{k}{n},\lambda_{2}-\tfrac{k}{n})

Let
\mathcal{D}_{k}\in\mathrm{Diff}_{\mathbb{C}}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\otimes\mathbb{C}^{k}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{n-1},y_{n}])
be the differential operator defined in
(
7.1
).

Theorem 10.6
.

Let
n\geq 2
.
Then we have

\mathrm{Diff}_{G}(I(\mathrm{triv};\bm{\lambda})^{\bm{\alpha}},I(\xi;\bm{\tau})^{\bm{\gamma}})=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{id}&\text{if $(\bm{\delta},\xi,\bm{\tau})=(\bm{\alpha},\mathrm{triv},\bm{\lambda})$,}\\
\mathbb{C}\mathcal{D}_{k}&\text{if $(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\delta};\xi;\bm{\lambda},\bm{\tau})\in\Lambda^{n+1}_{GL}$,}\\
\{0\}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

10.4.
Factorization identities of
\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}

We next state the factorization identities of
\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}
.

Theorem 10.7
.

Let
n\geq 2
.
Also,
let
\mathcal{D}_{\ell}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{D}_{k}
, and
\widetilde{\mathrm{Proj}}_{(m,\ell)}
be the same operators
considered in Theorem
7.18
.
Then, for

(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta};\varpi;\bm{\lambda},\bm{\nu})\in\Lambda^{(n+1,n)}_{GL,2}
,
the differential symmetry breaking operator

\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}
can be factored as in Theorem
7.18
, namely,

\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\ell}\circ\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}=\widetilde{\mathrm{Proj}}_{(m,\ell)}\circ\mathcal{D}_{m+\ell}.

Equivalently, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{I(\mathrm{triv};1-(m+\ell),\lambda_{2})^{(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{D}_{m+\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}}
\textstyle{J(\mathrm{triv};1-\ell,\lambda_{2})^{(\alpha_{1}+m,\alpha_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\circlearrowleft}
\textstyle{I(\mathrm{poly}^{m+\ell}_{n};1+\tfrac{m+\ell}{n},\lambda_{2}-\frac{m+\ell}{n})^{(\alpha_{1}+m+\ell,\alpha_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{Proj}}_{(m,\ell)}}
\scriptstyle{\circlearrowleft}
\textstyle{J(\mathrm{poly}^{\ell}_{n-1};1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1},\lambda_{2}-\tfrac{\ell}{n-1})^{(\alpha_{1}+m+\ell,\alpha_{2})}}

(10.8)

Remark 10.9
.

If
n=2
, then the factorization identity (
10.8
) becomes

\textstyle{I(\mathrm{triv};1-(m+\ell),\lambda_{2})^{(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{D}_{m+\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{D}_{(m,0)}}
\textstyle{J(\mathrm{triv};1-\ell,\lambda_{2})^{(\alpha_{1}+m,\alpha_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\circlearrowleft}
\textstyle{I(\mathrm{poly}^{m+\ell}_{2};1+\tfrac{m+\ell}{2},\lambda_{2}-\frac{m+\ell}{2})^{(\alpha_{1}+m+\ell,\alpha_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{Proj}}_{(m,\ell)}}
\scriptstyle{\circlearrowleft}
\textstyle{J(\mathrm{poly}^{\ell}_{1};1+\ell,\lambda_{2}-\ell)^{(\alpha_{1}+m+\ell,\alpha_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

=

\textstyle{J(\mathrm{triv};1+\ell,\lambda_{2}-\ell)^{(\alpha_{1}+m,\alpha_{2})}}

10.5.
Proofs of Theorems
10.3
,
10.6
, and
10.7

We only briefly discuss the proofs of Theorems

10.3
and
10.6
and
Theorem
10.7
as these are similar
to the ones of Theorems
4.15
and
7.18
.

10.5.1.
Proof of Theorem
10.3

The proofs of Theorems
10.3
and
10.6
utilize
the F-method. As the unipotent radicals
N_{\pm}
for the
GL
-case are the same as
those for the
SL
-case, the system of PDEs to solve is also essentially the same.
The only difference is the
M^{\prime}A^{\prime}
-decomposition

\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})|_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}=\mathbb{C}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n}]|_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}.

Thus, in the present subsection and the next, we only focus on Step 2 of the
recipe of the F-method.

As for the
SL
-case,
the following observation would play a role.

Lemma 10.10
.

The following hold.

(1)

(M^{\prime},\mathrm{Ad}_{\#},\mathbb{C}^{m}[\zeta_{n}])\hskip 42.0pt\simeq(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\times SL^{\pm}(n-1,\mathbb{R}),\mathrm{sgn}^{m}\boxtimes(\mathrm{triv}\otimes\mathrm{triv}),\mathbb{C})
.

(2)

(M^{\prime},\mathrm{Ad}_{\#},\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1}])\hskip 2.0pt\simeq(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\times SL^{\pm}(n-1,\mathbb{R}),\mathrm{sgn}^{\ell}\boxtimes(\mathrm{triv}\otimes\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1}),S^{\ell}(\mathbb{C}^{n-1}))
.

(3)

A_{1}^{\prime}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{m}[\zeta_{n}]
by a character with weight
-m
.

(4)

A_{1}^{\prime}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1}]
by a character
with weight
-\frac{n}{n-1}\ell
.

(4)

A_{2}^{\prime}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{m}[\zeta_{n}]
trivially.

(5)

A_{2}^{\prime}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1}]
by a character with weight

\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}
.

Proof.

A direct computation.
∎

Remark 10.11
.

As in Remark
5.6
, if
n=2
, then

\displaystyle(M^{\prime},\mathrm{Ad}_{\#},\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1}])

\displaystyle\simeq(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z},\mathrm{sgn}^{\ell}\boxtimes(\mathrm{triv}\otimes\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{1}),S^{\ell}(\mathbb{C}))

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z},\mathrm{triv}\boxtimes(\mathrm{triv}\otimes\mathrm{triv}),\mathbb{C}).

It follows from Lemma
10.10
that the decomposition

\mathbb{C}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n}]|_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}
is given as

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n}]|_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}

(10.12)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathbb{C}^{m}[\zeta_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1}]

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathrm{sgn}^{m+\ell}\boxtimes(\mathrm{triv}\otimes\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1})\boxtimes(-(m+\tfrac{n}{n-1}\ell),\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}),

(10.13)

where
(-(m+\tfrac{n}{n-1}\ell),\tfrac{\ell}{n-1})

indicates the weight of the character of
A^{\prime}=A_{1}^{\prime}A_{2}^{\prime}
.

As in (
5.4
), for
\bm{\alpha}=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\in\{\pm\}^{2}
, we write

\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\bm{\alpha}}=\mathbb{C}_{(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})}\otimes\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+}).

Then, for
\bm{\lambda}=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\big(\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\bm{\alpha}}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\bm{\lambda}}\big)|_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathrm{sgn}^{\alpha_{1}+(m+\ell)}\boxtimes(\mathrm{sgn}^{\alpha_{2}}\otimes\mathrm{sym}^{\ell}_{n-1})\boxtimes(-(\lambda_{1}+m+\tfrac{n}{n-1}\ell),-(\lambda_{2}-\tfrac{\ell}{n-1})).

(10.14)

We remark that the representations appeared in
(
10.14
) are all inequivalent even for
n=2
.

The rest of the arguments are proceeded exactly as in the proof of
Theorem
5.15
and Section
5.4
. Hence, we omit the details.

10.5.2.
Proof of Theorem
10.6

As in the previous section, we only focus on Step 2 of the recipe of the F-method.

Lemma 10.15
.

The following hold.

(1)

(M,\mathrm{Ad}_{\#},\mathbb{C}^{k}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n}])\hskip 2.0pt\simeq(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\times SL^{\pm}(n,\mathbb{R}),\mathrm{sgn}^{k}\boxtimes(\mathrm{triv}\otimes\mathrm{sym}^{k}_{n}),S^{k}(\mathbb{C}^{n}))
.

(2)

A_{1}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{k}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n}]
by a character
with weight
-\frac{n+1}{n}k
.

(3)

A_{2}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{k}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n}]

by a character with weight

\tfrac{k}{n}
.

Proof.

A direct computation.
∎

By Lemma
10.15
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n}]|_{MA}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathbb{C}^{k}[\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n-1},\zeta_{n}]

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathrm{sgn}^{k}\boxtimes(\mathrm{triv}\otimes\mathrm{sym}^{k}_{n})\boxtimes(-\tfrac{n+1}{n}k,\tfrac{k}{n}),

(10.16)

which shows that

\displaystyle\big(\mathrm{Pol}({\mathfrak{n}}_{+})_{\bm{\alpha}}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-\bm{\lambda}}\big)|_{MA}\simeq\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathrm{sgn}^{\alpha_{1}+k}\boxtimes(\mathrm{sgn}^{\alpha_{2}}\otimes\mathrm{sym}^{k}_{n})\boxtimes(-(\lambda_{1}+\tfrac{n+1}{n}k),-(\lambda_{2}-\tfrac{k}{n})).

(10.17)

Clearly, each irreducible constituent appeared in (
10.17
) is
inequivalent. The rest of the proof is essentially the same as

[
20
, Sect. 5]
or that of Theorem
5.15
.
Hence, we omit the details.

10.5.3.
Proof of Theorem
10.7

The proof of the factorization identities in Theorem
10.7

is also the same as that of Theorem
7.18
in principle.
The only thing that one needs to check is the
the linear map
\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}
satisfies
M^{\prime}A^{\prime}
-equivariance
also for the
GL
-case. Thus, in this section, we only show
the
M^{\prime}A^{\prime}
-equivariance of
\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}
.

Recall from (
7.8
) that, for
m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we define

\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}],\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}])

as

\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}:=\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}(e_{\mathbf{l}})^{\vee}\otimes e_{(m,\mathbf{l})}=\sum_{\mathbf{l}\in\Xi_{\ell}^{\prime}}\widetilde{y}_{\mathbf{l}}\otimes e_{(m,\mathbf{l})}.

For
\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
, we put

\bm{\mu}^{\prime}:=(1+\tfrac{\ell}{n-1},\lambda_{2}-\tfrac{\ell}{n-1})\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{\mu}:=(1+\tfrac{m+\ell}{n},\lambda_{2}-\tfrac{m+\ell}{n}).

Proposition 10.18
.

We have

\operatorname{Hom}_{M^{\prime}A^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]_{\bm{\alpha}}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\bm{\mu}^{\prime}},\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}]_{\bm{\alpha}}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\bm{\mu}})=\mathbb{C}\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}.

Proof.

As in the proof Proposition
7.10
, it suffices to show
that
\mathrm{Emb}_{(m,\ell)}
has the desired
A^{\prime}
-equivariance property.
For
A^{\prime}=A_{1}^{\prime}A_{2}^{\prime}
, the
A_{1}^{\prime}
-equivariance is already checked in
the proof of Proposition
7.10
. Thus, we only consider

A_{2}^{\prime}
-equivariance.

Take

b^{\prime}=\mathrm{diag}(1,t^{\frac{1}{n-1}},\ldots,t^{\frac{1}{n-1}},1)\in A_{2}^{\prime}.

The element
b^{\prime}
can be decomposed as

b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{M}b^{\prime}_{A}
with
b^{\prime}_{M}\in M
and
b^{\prime}_{A}\in A_{2}
, where

\displaystyle b^{\prime}_{M}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{diag}(1,t^{\frac{1}{n(n-1)}},\ldots,t^{\frac{1}{n(n-1)}},t^{\frac{-1}{n}})\in M,

\displaystyle b^{\prime}_{A}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{diag}(1,t^{\frac{1}{n}},\ldots,t^{\frac{1}{n}},t^{\frac{1}{n}})\in A_{2}.

As
\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\bm{\mu}^{\prime}}
is an
M^{\prime}A^{\prime}
-module by definition,

A^{\prime}_{2}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\bm{\mu}^{\prime}}
by a character
with weight
\lambda_{2}-\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}
.
Next, observe that the action of
M^{\prime}A^{\prime}

on
\mathbb{C}^{m}[e_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\bm{\mu}}

is the restriction of that of
MA
on
\mathbb{C}^{m+\ell}[e^{\prime},e_{n}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\bm{\mu}}
.
Thus, for
e_{n}^{m}p(e^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{1}_{-\bm{\mu}^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle b^{\prime}\cdot(e_{n}^{m}p(e^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{1}_{-\bm{\mu}})

\displaystyle=(b^{\prime}_{M}\cdot e_{n}^{m})(b^{\prime}_{M}\cdot p(e^{\prime}))\otimes(b^{\prime}_{A}\cdot\mathbb{1}_{-\bm{\mu}})

\displaystyle=t^{-\tfrac{m}{n}}\cdot t^{\tfrac{\ell}{n(n-1)}}\cdot t^{\lambda_{2}-\tfrac{m+\ell}{n}}(e_{n}^{m}p(e^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{1}_{-\bm{\mu}})

\displaystyle=t^{\lambda_{2}-\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}}(e_{n}^{m}p(e^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{1}_{-\bm{\mu}}).

Therefore,
A^{\prime}_{2}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{m}[e_{n}]\mathbb{C}^{\ell}[e^{\prime}]\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{-\bm{\mu}}

also by
\lambda_{2}-\tfrac{\ell}{n-1}
.
Now the proposition follows.
∎

Since the rest of the arguments is the same as those in Sections
7.2
and

7.3
, we omit the details.

Remark 10.19
.

As commented in Section
1.6
,
it follows from Theorem
10.3
that,
for
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})=(+,+)
,

m\in 2\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and
\ell\in 1+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
we have

\mathrm{Diff}_{G^{\prime}}(I(\mathrm{triv};1-(m+\ell),\lambda_{2})^{(+,+)},J(\mathrm{triv};1+\ell,\lambda_{2}-\ell)^{(+,+)}=\mathbb{C}\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)},

where

\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}=\mathrm{Rest}_{x_{n}=0}\circ\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial x_{2}^{m}}\frac{\partial^{\ell}}{\partial x_{1}^{\ell}}.

(10.20)

Since

\frac{\partial^{\ell}}{\partial x_{1}^{\ell}}
is the residue operators
of a Knapp–Stein operator,
the formula (
10.20
) shows that

\mathbb{D}_{(m,\ell)}
is a composition of
a normal derivative
\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial x_{2}^{m}}

and the residue operator
\frac{\partial^{\ell}}{\partial x_{1}^{\ell}}

of a Knapp–Stein operator.
